`code/mcm2/profile.c`:

```c
/*
    OpenMCM2 - the game's settings, kept in mcm2_profile.json beside it. See profile.h.
*/

#include "profile.h"
#include "runtime.h"

#include <intrin.h>

#define PROFILE_FILE "mcm2_profile.json"
#define GAME_KEY     "Software\\Microsoft\\Microsoft Games\\Motocross Madness 2"

#define MAX_NAME  256
#define MAX_KEY_PATH 512
#define MAX_VALUE 65536

static const char k_hex[] = "0123456789abcdef";

/* ------------------------------------------------------------------ reading the file */

typedef struct reader
{
    const char* at;
    const char* end;
} reader;

static void skip_space(reader* r)
{
    while (r->at < r->end && (*r->at == ' ' || *r->at == '\t' || *r->at == '\r' || *r->at == '\n'))
        ++r->at;
}

static BOOL expect(reader* r, char c)
{
    skip_space(r);
    if (r->at < r->end && *r->at == c)
    {
        ++r->at;
        return TRUE;
    }
    return FALSE;
}

/* A JSON string without \u escapes, which the profile never needs: names are ASCII. */
static BOOL read_string(reader* r, char* out, int size)
{
    int n = 0;

    if (!expect(r, '"'))
        return FALSE;

    while (r->at < r->end && *r->at != '"')
    {
        char c = *r->at++;

        if (c == '\\' && r->at < r->end)
            c = *r->at++;

        if (n >= size - 1)
            return FALSE;
        out[n++] = c;
    }
    out[n] = 0;
    return expect(r, '"');
}

static BOOL read_number(reader* r, DWORD* out)
{
    DWORD v = 0;

    skip_space(r);
    if (r->at >= r->end || *r->at < '0' || *r->at > '9')
        return FALSE;

    while (r->at < r->end && *r->at >= '0' && *r->at <= '9')
        v = v * 10 + (DWORD) (*r->at++ - '0');

    *out = v;
    return TRUE;
}

static int hex_digit(char c)
{
    if (c >= '0' && c <= '9') return c - '0';
    if (c >= 'a' && c <= 'f') return c - 'a' + 10;
    if (c >= 'A' && c <= 'F') return c - 'A' + 10;
    return -1;
}

/* A value's "hex bytes" string, decoded into `out`. */
static BOOL read_hex(reader* r, BYTE* out, DWORD size, DWORD* length)
{
    DWORD n = 0;

    if (!expect(r, '"'))
        return FALSE;

    while (r->at + 1 < r->end && *r->at != '"')
    {
        int hi = hex_digit(r->at[0]), lo = hex_digit(r->at[1]);

        if (hi < 0 || lo < 0 || n >= size)
            return FALSE;
        out[n++] = (BYTE) (hi << 4 | lo);
        r->at += 2;
    }
    *length = n;
    return expect(r, '"');
}

static void join_key(char* out, const char* subkey)
{
    lstrcpyA(out, GAME_KEY);
    if (*subkey)
    {
        int n = lstrlenA(out);
        out[n] = '\\';
        lstrcpyA(out + n + 1, subkey);
    }
}

/* One key's object: "name": [type, "hex"], ... */
static BOOL load_key(reader* r, HKEY key, BYTE* value)
{
    char name[MAX_NAME];
    DWORD type, length;

    if (!expect(r, '{'))
        return FALSE;
    if (expect(r, '}'))
        return TRUE;

    do
    {
        if (!read_string(r, name, sizeof name) || !expect(r, ':') || !expect(r, '[') ||
            !read_number(r, &type) || !expect(r, ',') || !read_hex(r, value, MAX_VALUE, &length) ||
            !expect(r, ']'))
            return FALSE;

        RegSetValueExA(key, name, 0, type, value, length);
    }
    while (expect(r, ','));

    return expect(r, '}');
}

static void load(void)
{
    HANDLE file = CreateFileA(PROFILE_FILE, GENERIC_READ, FILE_SHARE_READ, NULL, OPEN_EXISTING, 0, NULL);
    char subkey[MAX_NAME], path[MAX_KEY_PATH];
    DWORD size, got;
    char* text;
    BYTE* value;
    reader r;
    int keys = 0;

    if (file == INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE)
    {
        log_line("profile: no %s yet - the game starts from its defaults", PROFILE_FILE);
        return;
    }

    size = GetFileSize(file, NULL);
    text = (char*) VirtualAlloc(NULL, size + MAX_VALUE, MEM_COMMIT | MEM_RESERVE, PAGE_READWRITE);
    if (!text || !ReadFile(file, text, size, &got, NULL) || got != size)
    {
        CloseHandle(file);
        if (text)
            VirtualFree(text, 0, MEM_RELEASE);
        log_line("profile: could not read %s", PROFILE_FILE);
        return;
    }
    CloseHandle(file);

    value = (BYTE*) text + size;
    r.at = text;
    r.end = text + size;

    if (expect(&r, '{') && !expect(&r, '}'))
    {
        do
        {
            HKEY key;

            if (!read_string(&r, subkey, sizeof subkey) || !expect(&r, ':'))
                break;

            join_key(path, subkey);
            if (RegCreateKeyExA(HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE, path, 0, NULL, 0, KEY_WRITE, NULL, &key, NULL))
                break;

            if (!load_key(&r, key, value))
            {
                RegCloseKey(key);
                break;
            }
            RegCloseKey(key);
            ++keys;
        }
        while (expect(&r, ','));
    }

    if (r.at < r.end && !expect(&r, '}'))
        log_line("profile: %s is malformed after %d keys", PROFILE_FILE, keys);
    else
        log_line("profile: loaded %d keys from %s", keys, PROFILE_FILE);

    VirtualFree(text, 0, MEM_RELEASE);
}

/* ------------------------------------------------------------------ writing it back */

typedef LONG (WINAPI *enum_value_fn)(HKEY, DWORD, LPSTR, LPDWORD, LPDWORD, LPDWORD, LPBYTE, LPDWORD);

typedef struct writer
{
    char* text;
    DWORD length;
    DWORD capacity;
} writer;

static void put(writer* w, const char* s)
{
    while (*s && w->length < w->capacity)
        w->text[w->length++] = *s++;
}

/* A JSON string; only the backslash in key paths and quotes need escaping. */
static void put_string(writer* w, const char* s)
{
    char c[2] = { 0, 0 };

    put(w, "\"");
    for (; *s; ++s)
    {
        if (*s == '\\' || *s == '"')
            put(w, "\\");
        c[0] = *s;
        put(w, c);
    }
    put(w, "\"");
}

static void lower(char* s)
{
    for (; *s; ++s)
    {
        if (*s >= 'A' && *s <= 'Z')
            *s += 'a' - 'A';
    }
}

/* One key's values, as the launcher writes them: lower-case names, json.dump(indent=1). */
static void save_values(writer* w, HKEY key, enum_value_fn enum_value, BYTE* value)
{
    char name[MAX_NAME], number[16];
    DWORD i, name_size, type, length, b;
    int first = 1;

    put(w, "{");
    for (i = 0;; ++i)
    {
        name_size = sizeof name;
        length = MAX_VALUE;
        if (enum_value(key, i, name, &name_size, NULL, &type, value, &length))
            break;

        lower(name);
        put(w, first ? "\n  " : ",\n  ");
        first = 0;
        put_string(w, name);
        wsprintfA(number, ": [\n   %u,\n   \"", type);
        put(w, number);
        for (b = 0; b < length; ++b)
        {
            char pair[3] = { k_hex[value[b] >> 4], k_hex[value[b] & 15], 0 };
            put(w, pair);
        }
        put(w, "\"\n  ]");
    }
    put(w, first ? "}" : "\n }");
}

/* `subkey` and everything below it, each as its own entry. */
static void save_key(writer* w, const char* subkey, enum_value_fn enum_value, BYTE* value, int* entries)
{
    char path[MAX_KEY_PATH], child[MAX_NAME], below[MAX_KEY_PATH];
    HKEY key;
    DWORD i;

    join_key(path, subkey);
    if (RegOpenKeyExA(HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE, path, 0, KEY_READ, &key))
        return;

    put(w, *entries ? ",\n " : "\n ");
    put_string(w, subkey);
    put(w, ": ");
    save_values(w, key, enum_value, value);
    ++*entries;

    for (i = 0; !RegEnumKeyA(key, i, child, sizeof child); ++i)
    {
        lstrcpyA(below, subkey);
        if (*subkey)
            lstrcpyA(below + lstrlenA(below), "\\");
        lstrcpyA(below + lstrlenA(below), child);
        save_key(w, below, enum_value, value, entries);
    }
    RegCloseKey(key);
}

static void save(void)
{
    typedef BOOL (WINAPI *move_fn)(LPCSTR, LPCSTR, DWORD);
    enum_value_fn enum_value = (enum_value_fn) api("advapi32.dll", "RegEnumValueA");
    move_fn move = (move_fn) api("kernel32.dll", "MoveFileExA");
    writer w;
    BYTE* value;
    HANDLE file;
    DWORD written;
    int entries = 0;

    if (!enum_value || !move)
        return;

    w.capacity = 4 << 20;
    w.length = 0;
    w.text = (char*) VirtualAlloc(NULL, w.capacity + MAX_VALUE, MEM_COMMIT | MEM_RESERVE, PAGE_READWRITE);
    if (!w.text)
        return;
    value = (BYTE*) w.text + w.capacity;

    put(&w, "{");
    save_key(&w, "", enum_value, value, &entries);
    put(&w, "\n}");

    /* Written beside it and then moved over it, so an interrupted save cannot lose the profile. */
    file = CreateFileA(PROFILE_FILE ".new", GENERIC_WRITE, 0, NULL, CREATE_ALWAYS, 0, NULL);
    if (entries && w.length < w.capacity && file != INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE &&
        WriteFile(file, w.text, w.length, &written, NULL) && written == w.length)
    {
        CloseHandle(file);
        if (move(PROFILE_FILE ".new", PROFILE_FILE, MOVEFILE_REPLACE_EXISTING))
            log_line("profile: saved %d keys to %s", entries, PROFILE_FILE);
    }
    else if (file != INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE)
    {
        CloseHandle(file);
        DeleteFileA(PROFILE_FILE ".new");
    }

    VirtualFree(w.text, 0, MEM_RELEASE);
}

/* ------------------------------------------------------------------ installing */

typedef void (WINAPI *exit_fn)(UINT);

extern exit_fn _imp__ExitProcess;       /* the game's own import slot */
static exit_fn g_exit;

static void WINAPI exit_process(UINT code)
{
    static volatile long saving;

    if (!_InterlockedExchange(&saving, 1))
        save();
    g_exit(code);
}

void profile_install(void)
{
    exit_fn mine = exit_process;

    load();

    g_exit = _imp__ExitProcess;
    if (!write_protected(&_imp__ExitProcess, &mine, sizeof mine))
        log_line("profile: could not take over ExitProcess - settings will not be saved");
}

```

/*
    OpenMCM2 - the game's settings, kept in mcm2_profile.json beside it.

    The game keeps its settings, profiles and EULA acceptance under HKLM\Software\Microsoft\
    Microsoft Games\Motocross Madness 2 (virtualised to the user's VirtualStore). The portable
    copy's launcher carries them in mcm2_profile.json and loads them into the registry before the
    game starts; this does the same, and writes them back when the game exits.

    The file holds one object per key, named by its path below the game's key ("" for the key
    itself), and each value as [type, "hex bytes"].
*/

#pragma once

/* Load mcm2_profile.json into the registry, and save it back when the game calls ExitProcess. */
void profile_install(void);

`code/mcm2/render.h`:

```h
/*
    OpenMCM2 - the renderer: DirectDraw 7 and Direct3D 7, as much of them as the game uses, on OpenGL.

    The game gets these objects from DirectDrawCreateEx, which is taken over at start-up, so its
    own rendering code runs unchanged. What it uses was measured, not guessed: a hardware device,
    16-bit textures, no lights, TL and LVERTEX geometry with fog in specular alpha, GDI text, Blt
    and BltFast for 2D, and Flip.
    Anything else answers DDERR_UNSUPPORTED and is logged the first time.

    Render targets (the back buffer, and any 3D offscreen surface) are framebuffer objects at the
    game's own resolution, so 2D work on them - Lock, GetDC, Blt - is pixel-exact. Flip scales the
    back buffer into the window, keeping its aspect. Every other surface lives in memory as the game
    writes it, and a texture is uploaded when it changes.
*/

#pragma once

#define DIRECTDRAW_VERSION 0x0700
#define DIRECT3D_VERSION   0x0700
#define CINTERFACE

#include "gl.h"

#include <ddraw.h>
#include <d3d.h>

/* The 16-bit formats the renderer offers; the game picks its textures from these. */
enum pixel_format { PF_565, PF_1555, PF_4444, PF_FORMATS };

typedef struct surface
{
    IDirectDrawSurface7  iface;
    IDirectDrawSurface3  iface1;        /* versions 1-3, for DirectShow's video streams: see ddraw1.c */
    IDirectDrawGammaControl gamma;
    LONG                 refs;
    DDSURFACEDESC2       desc;          /* as the game sees it */
    int                  format;        /* pixel_format, for colour surfaces */

    BYTE*                pixels;        /* the surface in memory: textures, offscreen, a locked target */
    BOOL                 locked_target; /* a render target's pixels hold a Lock or GetDC readback */

    GLuint               fbo, colour, depth;    /* a render target */
    GLuint               texture;       /* a texture, uploaded when `dirty` */
    BOOL                 dirty;

    struct surface*      back;          /* the primary's back buffer */
    struct surface*      mip;           /* the next smaller mipmap level */
    struct surface*      zbuffer;       /* attached with AddAttachedSurface */
    struct surface*      top;           /* the level that owns the mip chain, or itself */

    DDCOLORKEY           key;           /* source colour key, if `keyed` */
    BOOL                 keyed;

    HDC                  dc;            /* GetDC: a DIB section over a copy of the pixels */
    HBITMAP              dib, old_bitmap;
    void*                dib_bits;

    IDirectDrawPalette*  palette;
} surface;

typedef struct ddraw
{
    IDirectDraw7 iface;
    IDirectDraw  iface1;                /* for DirectShow's video streams: see ddraw1.c */
    LONG         refs;
    IDirect3D7*  d3d;
} ddraw;

/* ------------------------------------------------------------------ ddraw.c */

/* The surface behind an IDirectDrawSurface7 the game hands back, or NULL for one that is not ours. */
surface* surface_of(IDirectDrawSurface7* iface);

/* ------------------------------------------------------------------ ddraw1.c */

/* The version-1 interfaces, which forward to the version-7 ones. */
extern IDirectDrawVtbl        k_ddraw1_vtbl;
extern IDirectDrawSurface3Vtbl k_surface1_vtbl;

/* The surface behind a version 1-3 interface, or NULL. */
surface* surface_of1(IDirectDrawSurface3* iface);

/* Bytes per row of a surface's pixels. */
LONG surface_pitch(const surface* s);

/* The DDPIXELFORMAT of one of the renderer's formats. */
void describe_format(int format, DDPIXELFORMAT* pf);

/* Make a render target the one OpenGL draws into, creating its framebuffer on first use. */
void target_bind(surface* target);

/* Bind a texture surface to the active texture unit, uploading it if the game changed it. */
void texture_bind(surface* texture);

/* The game's display mode, as it set it - the size its full-screen render targets have. */
void display_size(DWORD* width, DWORD* height);

/* 2D work outside the device changed OpenGL's state: the device must set its own again. */
extern BOOL g_state_lost;

/* ------------------------------------------------------------------ device.c */

/* Hand the game this renderer instead of DirectDraw. */
void render_install(void);

/* The Direct3D7 object that belongs to a DirectDraw object, created on first QueryInterface. */
IDirect3D7* direct3d_create(IDirectDraw7* owner);

/* The log line for a method nobody has implemented, once per method. */
HRESULT unsupported(const char* method);

/* A QueryInterface the renderer turns down: logged, so a missing interface shows in the log. */
HRESULT no_interface(const char* object, REFIID iid, void** out);

```

`code/mcm2/runtime.c`:

```c
/*
    OpenMCM2 - runtime support: the log, runtime-resolved functions, and protected writes.
*/

#include "runtime.h"

#include <intrin.h>
#include <stdarg.h>

/* The compiler marks any object that uses floating point by referring to this; the CRT that
   normally defines it is not linked. */
int _fltused = 0x9875;

static HANDLE g_log = INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE;

void log_open(void)
{
    typedef BOOL (WINAPI *move_fn)(LPCSTR, LPCSTR, DWORD);
    move_fn move = (move_fn) api("kernel32.dll", "MoveFileExA");

    /* The previous run's log is kept, so a quick relaunch cannot wipe what a long session found. */
    if (move)
        move("openmcm2.log", "openmcm2.prev.log", MOVEFILE_REPLACE_EXISTING);

    g_log = CreateFileA("openmcm2.log", GENERIC_WRITE, FILE_SHARE_READ, NULL, CREATE_ALWAYS,
                        FILE_ATTRIBUTE_NORMAL, NULL);
}

void log_line(const char* fmt, ...)
{
    char line[1024];
    DWORD written;
    va_list args;
    int n;

    if (g_log == INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE)
        return;

    va_start(args, fmt);
    n = wvsprintfA(line, fmt, args);    /* stops at 1024 characters, the size of the buffer */
    va_end(args);

    if (n < 0)
        return;

    if (n > (int) sizeof line - 3)
        n = (int) sizeof line - 3;

    line[n++] = '\r';
    line[n++] = '\n';
    WriteFile(g_log, line, (DWORD) n, &written, NULL);
}

FARPROC api(const char* dll, const char* name)
{
    HMODULE module = LoadLibraryA(dll);

    return module ? GetProcAddress(module, name) : NULL;
}

static HANDLE heap(void)
{
    typedef HANDLE (WINAPI *heap_fn)(void);
    static HANDLE process_heap;

    if (!process_heap)
        process_heap = ((heap_fn) api("kernel32.dll", "GetProcessHeap"))();
    return process_heap;
}

void* mem_alloc(SIZE_T size)
{
    return HeapAlloc(heap(), HEAP_ZERO_MEMORY, size);
}

void mem_free(void* p)
{
    if (p)
        HeapFree(heap(), 0, p);
}

BOOL write_protected(void* to, const void* from, SIZE_T size)
{
    typedef BOOL (WINAPI *protect_fn)(void*, SIZE_T, DWORD, DWORD*);
    static protect_fn protect;
    DWORD old;

    if (!protect)
        protect = (protect_fn) api("kernel32.dll", "VirtualProtect");

    /* Execute stays on: a vtable can share its page with code. */
    if (!protect || !protect(to, size, PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE, &old))
        return FALSE;

    __movsb((unsigned char*) to, (const unsigned char*) from, size);
    protect(to, size, old, &old);
    return TRUE;
}

```

`code/mcm2/runtime.h`:

```h
/*
    OpenMCM2 - runtime support for the code compiled into the image.

    There is no CRT here and no import table of our own: Win32 calls go through the game's own
    import slots (see tools/link.py), and anything the game does not import is looked up at
    runtime with api().
*/

#pragma once

#define WIN32_LEAN_AND_MEAN
#include <windows.h>

/*
    PORT(0x0044C1D0) on the line before a function definition makes it replace the game's function
    at that address (tools/port.py). The original stays callable as game_orig_<its name>.

    The calling convention must be the original's exactly. A C++ method (__thiscall: `this` in ECX,
    the callee pops the arguments) is written as __fastcall with an unused second parameter, which
    takes EDX:  int __fastcall widget_count(widget_list* self, void* edx_unused).
*/
#define PORT(address)

/* openmcm2.log in the game folder; the previous run's is kept as openmcm2.prev.log. */
void log_open(void);
void log_line(const char* fmt, ...);

/* A function the game does not import, or NULL. */
FARPROC api(const char* dll, const char* name);

/* Zeroed memory from the process heap, and its release. */
void* mem_alloc(SIZE_T size);
void  mem_free(void* p);

/* Copy into memory the loader made read-only, such as the import table or a vtable. */
BOOL write_protected(void* to, const void* from, SIZE_T size);

```

`code/mcm2/start.c`:

```c
/*
    OpenMCM2 - the image's entry point.

    Installs whatever replaces part of the game, then runs the game's own start-up, which never
    returns: the 1999 CRT ends the process itself.

    -ddraw on the command line keeps the original DirectDraw, traced, for comparison.
*/

#include "runtime.h"
#include "ddtrace.h"
#include "profile.h"
#include "render.h"

void game_entry(void);

static BOOL has_switch(const char* name)
{
    const char* line = GetCommandLineA();
    int n = lstrlenA(name);

    for (; *line; ++line)
    {
        if ((line[0] == '-' || line[0] == '/') && CompareStringA(LOCALE_INVARIANT, NORM_IGNORECASE, line + 1, n, name, n) == CSTR_EQUAL)
            return TRUE;
    }
    return FALSE;
}

void open_start(void)
{
    log_open();
    log_line("OpenMCM2 starting");
    profile_install();

    if (has_switch("ddraw"))
        ddtrace_install();
    else
        render_install();

    game_entry();
}

```

`docs/assets.md`:

```md
# Asset formats

Separate from the executable work, and not started yet. Recorded here so the groundwork is not
redone later.

## What the install contains

`Desktop\PC Games\MCM2` holds the game data in per-mode directories - `BAJA`, `ENDURO`,
`NATIONAL`, `QUARRIES`, `SX`, `TAG`, `TERAFORM` (the track editor's data), plus `BIKES`, `RIDERS`,
`UI` and `RECORD`. Roughly 1.5 GB in total.

## A published reference exists

**https://github.com/AlexKimov/motocross-madness-file-formats** (MIT, archived read-only since
2023-09-15, 8 commits). It carries 010 Editor binary templates (`.bt`) and scripts (`.1sc`) rather
than prose specifications:

| | |
|---|---|
| MCM2 | `DAT` game resources - `DecodeRES.1sc`, `unpackDAT2.1sc` |
| MCM1 | `DAT` resources (`unpackDAT.1sc`), `TRN` track data, `VUB` animations |

Only the `DAT` container is covered for MCM2; MCM1's track and animation formats are documented but
MCM2's are not, so its `TRN` template is a starting point rather than an answer.

Two things to keep in mind when it is picked up:

- **A 010 template is a parser, not a specification.** It encodes field offsets and sizes, which is
  most of what is needed, but nothing about meaning or invariants. Read it as evidence, then confirm
  against the binary - `Mcm2.exe`'s own loader is the authority, and after the RTTI pass there are
  named classes to read it from.
- **It describes the retail formats.** This port targets a specific patched image (see
  [`binary.md`](binary.md)); nothing suggests the data files differ, but that is an assumption until
  a file is round-tripped.

The executable side does not depend on any of this. It matters for the modding and asset work that
follows a running binary.

```

`docs/binary.md`:

```md
# The target binary

Everything here was read out of the file, not inferred. Where a number matters to the port, the
tool that produced it is named so it can be re-run.

## Which file is the target, and why

The install at `Desktop\PC Games\MCM2` holds three executables. Only one is analysable:

| file | size | what it is |
|---|--:|---|
| `Mcm2.exe` | 1,577,005 | **the target.** A clean MSVC 6.0 PE32 with real code, RTTI and a sane entry point. |
| `MCM2.ICD` | 1,577,005 | the SafeDisc payload. Same length as `Mcm2.exe` and **1,463,835 of its bytes differ** - 93% - so it is still encrypted. Nothing can be recovered from it. |
| `MCM2-original.EXE` | 252,856 | the original SafeDisc loader stub. Not the game. |

`Mcm2.exe` is therefore the decrypted image, and it is what the game actually runs.

    sha256  cfcffdca19314b630954c5dbd0003cd1dd4a7c6b63d4a5739cc293af0bcadda4

**Every address in this project is valid only for that hash.** The copy under
`C:\mcm2ghidra\bin\Mcm2.exe` is byte-identical and is what Ghidra imports; the Ghidra workspace is
deliberately outside OneDrive and space-free, because OneDrive corrupts Ghidra project files and
`analyzeHeadless.bat` re-splits arguments on spaces.

One cosmetic tell that this image was patched: the PE timestamp is `0x6B637566`, which is the ASCII
`fuck` little-endian. It is a cracker's stamp, it has no effect on the code, and it is recorded here
only so nobody later mistakes it for a meaningful date.

## PE headers

Measured by `tools/pedump.py`.

| | |
|---|---|
| machine | `0x014C` i386 |
| optional header | `0x010B` PE32 |
| linker | **6.0** (Visual C++ 6.0) |
| image base | `0x00400000` |
| entry | RVA `0x0013D6F5`, VA **`0x0053D6F5`** |
| section alignment | `0x1000` |
| file alignment | **`0x1000`** |
| size of image | `0x296000` |
| subsystem | 2, WINDOWS_GUI |
| characteristics | `0x010F` = RELOCS_STRIPPED, EXECUTABLE_IMAGE, LINE_NUMS_STRIPPED, LOCAL_SYMS_STRIPPED, 32BIT_MACHINE |

Note the file alignment is `0x1000`, not the usual `0x200`. It matters when reasoning about where a
section's raw bytes sit relative to its virtual address.

### Sections

| name | VA | virtual size | raw ptr | raw size |
|---|---|--:|---|--:|
| `.text` | `0x00401000` | `0x153A7E` | `0x00001000` | `0x154000` |
| `.rdata` | `0x00555000` | `0x01646F` | `0x00155000` | `0x017000` |
| `.data` | `0x0056C000` | `0x1265E0` | `0x0016C000` | `0x012000` |
| `.rsrc` | `0x00693000` | `0x002286` | `0x0017E000` | `0x003000` |

`.text` is 1,391,230 bytes. For scale, MM2's is about 1.76 MB, so this is roughly four fifths the
size of a port that reached a linking binary - the "smaller game" expectation is borne out, but not
by an order of magnitude.

`.data`'s virtual size (`0x1265E0`) is fifteen times its raw size (`0x12000`); the remainder is
uninitialised BSS that the loader zero-fills.

There is **no `.idata` section** - the import table lives inside `.rdata`, exactly as in MM2. That
is the same condition that leaves MM2's `.data` sitting `0x3000` high, because the linker builds its
own import table and places it in `.rdata` on top of the original. Expect to meet it here too.

## The relocation problem is identical to MM2's

`RELOCS_STRIPPED` is set **and** the base-relocation data directory is absent (RVA 0, size 0). So:

- The image can only ever load at `0x400000`; the link must use `-FIXED -BASE:0x400000`.
- Nothing in the file says which four-byte words in `.data` are pointers and which are integers or
  floats that merely look like addresses. Code operands are recoverable because Ghidra's analysis
  determines from context which are addresses; data words are not, and must be treated
  conservatively. `docs/harness.md` in the OpenMM2 tree records what that cost and the four rules
  that were tried before one worked.

This is the single most important structural fact, and it is why the OpenMM2 harness design carries
over unchanged rather than needing a new one.

## Imports

Measured by `tools/imports.py`. **15 DLLs, 244 functions.**

| DLL | funcs | by ordinal | IAT VA |
|---|--:|--:|---|
| `KERNEL32.dll` | 121 | 0 | `0x005550C0` |
| `USER32.dll` | 54 | 0 | `0x005552BC` |
| `GDI32.dll` | 18 | 0 | `0x00555044` |
| `IMM32.dll` | 11 | 0 | `0x00555090` |
| `WSOCK32.dll` | 10 | **10** | `0x005553BC` |
| `ADVAPI32.dll` | 9 | 0 | `0x00555000` |
| `WINMM.dll` | 8 | 0 | `0x00555398` |
| `ole32.dll` | 3 | 0 | `0x005553FC` |
| `blade.dll` | 2 | 0 | `0x005553E8` |
| `MSVFW32.dll` | 2 | 0 | `0x005552A8` |
| `DSOUND.dll` | 2 | **2** | `0x00555038` |
| `SHELL32.dll` | 1 | 0 | `0x005552B4` |
| `d3drm.dll` | 1 | 0 | `0x005553F4` |
| `DINPUT.dll` | 1 | 0 | `0x00555028` |
| `DPLAYX.dll` | 1 | **1** | `0x00555030` |

Three of these would matter to an import library - 13 imports are by ordinal with no name (all of
`WSOCK32`, both of `DSOUND`, the single `DPLAYX` entry), `DPLAYX.dll` and `DINPUT.dll` are gone from
the modern SDK, and `blade.dll` is the game's own - but none is needed any more. The build links with
no import libraries at all and uses retail's import table as it stands (see
[`harness.md`](harness.md)), which carries every one of these correctly already.

`d3drm.dll` is Direct3D Retained Mode, and `DDraw.dll` / `D3DImm.dll` / `d3drm.dll` in the install
folder are dgVoodoo wrappers, not the Microsoft originals. Retail runs on them; OpenMCM2 does not
load them, since its own renderer answers `DirectDrawCreateEx`.

## Toolchain present on this machine

| | |
|---|---|
| `ml.exe`, `link.exe`, `lib.exe` | `C:\Program Files\Microsoft Visual Studio\18\Community\VC\Tools\MSVC\14.51.36231\bin\Hostx64\x86` |
| Ghidra | `Desktop\Dev Workspace\ghidra_12.1.2_PUBLIC` |
| Python | 3.12 (default) and 3.10 |

Two environment traps, both already paid for on the MM2 port and both still live here: the headless
launcher re-splits arguments on spaces, so every path handed to it must be absolute and space-free;
and Git Bash rewrites `/c`-style flags into `C:/`, so MASM must be driven with dash flags
(`-c -coff`) rather than slash flags.

```

`docs/function_recovery.md`:

```md
# Ghidra misses a quarter of this binary's functions, and why

This is the first substantive finding, and it had to be settled before anything else, because a
function that is not in the database cannot be exported with symbolised operands, cannot be named,
and cannot be ported.

## The symptom

A census of the freshly analysed database:

```
functions          : 4170  (51 thunks)
.text              : 1392640 bytes
  covered by funcs : 1275513  (91.59%)
  NOT covered      : 117127 in 1070 runs  (8.41%)
```

117 KB of executable section belonging to no function, in runs as long as 5,984 bytes. A jump table
that size would hold 1,496 entries, so the large runs were never plausibly tables.

## What is in the gaps

`Gaps.java` classifies every uncovered byte instead of guessing:

```
uncovered .text: 117127 bytes in 1070 runs
   already instructions :   100284  (85.6%)
   defined data         :     2497  (2.1%)
   UNDEFINED bytes      :    14346  (12.2%)
   'push ebp; mov ebp,esp' sequences inside them: 0
```

The last line is the finding. **Not one frame-pointer prologue in 117 KB.** The runs instead open
like this:

| bytes | meaning |
|---|---|
| `64 A1 00 00 00 00` | `mov eax, fs:[0]` - the MSVC SEH frame setup |
| `83 EC 20` / `81 EC AC 00 00 00` | `sub esp, N` - a frame-pointer-omitted prologue |
| `8B 44 24 04` / `8A 44 24 04` | `mov eax, [esp+4]` - reading an argument directly |
| `8B 41 04 C3` | `mov eax, [ecx+4]; ret` - a thiscall accessor |
| `B8 01 00 00 00 C2 04 00` | `mov eax, 1; ret 4` - a virtual returning true |

**`Mcm2.exe` was built by VC6 at `/O2`, which implies `/Oy` - frame pointer omission.** Ghidra's
MSVC function-start patterns are built around `push ebp; mov ebp, esp`, so they match none of these.
Nothing is damaged or hidden; the functions simply do not look like what was searched for.

That explains the shape. It does not explain why a call-reference search finds them either - and it
does not: **zero** of the uncovered runs are the target of a `call`. They are reached through
vtables, so no direct call instruction leads to any of them.

## The rule that works, and the two that did not

The first attempt walked each uncovered run, skipped leading padding and looked for a prologue. It
was wrong in three ways at once, and all three matter:

1. **VC6 pads with multi-byte nops.** `8B FF` (`mov edi, edi`) and `8D 49 00` (`lea ecx, [ecx+0]`)
   are alignment padding, and a skip that only knows `0x90` and `0xCC` walks straight past the end
   of the padding into what it thinks is the function.
2. **A run usually holds several consecutive undiscovered functions.** Examining only the first
   address in a 5,696-byte run can never find the other twenty.
3. **It cannot tell a function from a jump table.** Several runs are plainly tables - ascending
   dwords such as `DF 7D 42 00, FD 7D 42 00, 37 7E 42 00` - and disassembling a jump table as code
   writes damage into the database that no later rule change can undo, because the evidence it would
   need has been overwritten.

The rule that replaced it involves no padding, no prologue and no run boundaries:

> Every dword in the image that points into `.text` is a candidate. If no function covers that
> address, it is a function start.

This is proof rather than resemblance. A dword holding a `.text` address is a vtable slot, a
function-table entry or a switch target - and a switch target already lies inside its own function's
body, so the same test excludes it. Measured over the whole image:

```
dwords scanned            : 673144
pointing into .text       : 10899  (4071 distinct)
   already a function entry : 1282
   inside a known body      : 1194  (switch targets and internal labels)
   COVERED BY NOTHING       : 1595  <- undiscovered functions
```

## Creating them, in two tiers

Not every dword that looks like a `.text` address is one. `0x00404031` appears in the candidate list
and opens `11 8B 56 48` - an `adc` - which is a coincidence, not a function. Creating a function
there would decode real bytes at the wrong offset. So creation is split, and the two halves are
counted separately so their risk is visible:

- **Tier A** - the address is *already* the start of a disassembled instruction. Nothing new is
  decoded; a function is simply drawn round bytes Ghidra had already ruled were code. No risk.
- **Tier B** - undefined bytes, which must be disassembled. Two guards: Ghidra refuses to
  disassemble over an existing instruction, so a mid-instruction address rejects itself; and the
  byte *before* a real function start is always a terminator or padding (`90 CC C3 C2 E9 EB 00`),
  never live code, so anything else is skipped untouched.

```
tier A - already code, function drawn round it : 1104
tier B - undefined bytes disassembled          :   48
absorbed by a function made earlier            :  370
skipped, previous byte is live code            :   24
refused by the disassembler                    :   49
```

73 candidates were refused rather than forced. That is the right outcome: in a binary with no
relocation table there is no recovering from a wrong guess written into the database.

## Result

| | before | after |
|---|--:|--:|
| functions | 4,170 | **5,658** |
| thunks | 51 | 401 |
| `.text` covered | 91.59% | **98.54%** |
| uncovered bytes | 117,127 | **20,322** |
| largest uncovered run | 5,984 | **1,001** |

The remaining 1.46% is padding and genuine jump tables, which is what should be left.

`reimport.sh` runs this pass as part of building the database, because a database without it is a
different database and every later measurement would silently depend on which one was in use.

## What is still owed

4,410 of the 5,658 functions are still `FUN_*`. Names have to come from RTTI - this binary does
carry MSVC RTTI, unlike MM2 - and from the string and call-graph work that follows.

```

`docs/harness.md`:

```md
# The assemble-and-link harness, ported from OpenMM2

The design is not restated here - `OpenMM2/docs/harness.md` is the spec, and it holds unchanged
because `Mcm2.exe` has the same shape in every respect that matters (see [`binary.md`](binary.md)).
This records what the port needed, which is only interesting where MCM2 differs.

In short: emit the whole original executable as MASM, link it, and get a runnable binary at 0%
reimplemented. Then port one function at a time - write the C++, flip `IMPORT` to `EXPORT`, strip
that `PROC` out of `game.asm`, rebuild, run. Each function is verified against the real game the day
it is written, and "how much is left" is literally the size of `game.asm`.

## What the exporter needed

`ExportAsm.java` came across almost intact. Its output directory is already a script argument, and
none of the MM2 addresses in it are load-bearing - they appear only in comments. So the port was the
default path, the category tag, and three real defects.

**All three were caused by the same difference: `Mcm2.exe` carries MSVC RTTI and `midtown2.exe` does
not.** RTTI gives Ghidra 256 type descriptors, 253 vtables and a set of demangled names, and those
names break assumptions the MM2 exporter was never asked to hold.

### 1. `~` is not a MASM identifier character

Ghidra names a destructor `~Name`. `ml.exe` answers with

```
game.asm(492524) : error A2044:invalid character in file
... 100 consecutive lines ...
game.asm(492548) : fatal error A1012:error count exceeds 100; stopping assembly
```

None of the reported lines is wrong, none of them contains the offending character, and the message
names neither the symbol nor the character. The whole of it was one symbol - `~exception`, six lines
out of 21 MB.

`sanitize()` now replaces anything outside MASM's set (`A-Z a-z 0-9 _ $ ? @ .`), with a leading `~`
becoming `dtor_` so the name still says what it is. Mangled names starting with `?` are returned
untouched, as before: they are the interface the ported C++ links against, and they are legal
already.

Scanning the whole 21 MB for illegal identifiers - in labels, `EXTERN`s and operands alike - found
exactly one, which is worth knowing because it means the rule is cheap.

### 2. Reading the raw symbol map instead of the disambiguated one

Ghidra reuses a name freely across addresses. With RTTI present, `RTTI_Type_Descriptor` occurs
**256 times** and `vftable` likewise. `assignUniqueNames()` already resolves that - it is where the
"1,294 disambiguated" in the log comes from - and writes the result to `finalName`, which `nameFor()`
reads.

The exporter read `symbolAt` **directly in ten places**, bypassing it, and emitted 256 identical
labels:

```
game.asm(566995) : error A2005:symbol redefinition : RTTI_Type_Descriptor
```

Every site that turns an address into emitted text now goes through `nameFor()`; only
`buildSymbolIndex`, `assignUniqueNames` and `nameFor` itself touch the raw map. `nameFor()` carries
a comment saying so, because the next person to add an emit site will otherwise reach for the map.

This one deserves a note beyond the fix. **The bug was harmless in MM2 purely by luck** - its data
symbols happened to be unique. And it failed loudly here only because duplicate *definitions* are an
assembly error. The same bug in a *reference* would have quietly resolved to whichever duplicate the
assembler saw first, which is the failure mode the OpenMM2 notes describe as invisible to a
differential verifier: the bytes differ from retail by exactly the amount a correct relocation
would.

### 3. A label that has to land inside a pointer

`.rdata` holds a `.text` pointer at `0x5555FC`, and the code does `push offset D_5555FF` - a pointer
to that pointer's own last byte, which happens to be zero. It is an ordinary VC6 economy: rather
than allocate an empty string, the compiler points at a zero byte it already has.

Emitted as a single `dd offset ...` there is nowhere to put the label, so it was never written and
the two instructions using it failed with `A2006: undefined symbol : D_5555FF`.

The pointer branch now declines when a needed label falls at `a+1`, `a+2` or `a+3`, and the dword
falls through to byte emission with the label between the bytes. This is the data counterpart of a
rule `.text` already had - *an instruction with a label inside it keeps its bytes* - and the count is
reported per export.

The trade is that such a dword keeps its literal retail value rather than being relocated. Here that
is exactly right, because it points into `.text` and the link places `.text` at its original
address. A pointer into `.rdata` or `.data` would be wrong by that section's displacement, which is
why the number is printed rather than passed over in silence.

## Export figures

From the first clean export of `Mcm2.exe`:

| | |
|---|--:|
| functions emitted | 5,720 |
| absolute relocations | 22,530 |
| relative relocations | 29,013 |
| import relocations | 959 |
| data pointers symbolised | 10,987 |
| data labels emitted | 6,529 |
| **references with no symbol** | **1** |
| imports | 244 |
| instructions | 388,681 |
| `game.asm` | 21.2 MB |

One unresolved reference in the whole binary, against three for MM2.

Everything is currently emitted as `db`. That is the correct bootstrap state: `verify_encodings.py`
derives the mnemonic table *from* an export, so the order is export-as-bytes, measure, re-export.
`ExportAsm` handles the missing table on its own ("no encodings.tsv next to the output - emitting
all instructions as db").

## It links, and every byte of .text is accounted for

`sh C:/mcm2ghidra/cycle.sh` runs export, assemble, link and verify as one command - together on
purpose, because doing them separately twice let a stale executable be measured as if it were fresh,
and that failure is silent: the stale build is internally consistent, so every gate passes.

```
game.obj          3,339,479 bytes, assembled with zero errors
OpenMCM2.exe      2,713,088 bytes, linked with zero errors
```

| | retail | built |
|---|---|---|
| entry RVA | `0x0013D6F5` | **`0x0013D6F5`** |
| `.text` VA | `0x00401000` | **`0x00401000`** |
| `.rdata` VA | `0x00555000` | **`0x00555000`** |
| `.data` VA | `0x0056C000` | `0x00571000` (+0x5000) |

`tools/verify_text.py` compares **all 1,391,230 bytes** of `.text` against retail and demands an
account of every difference:

```
differing bytes: 34982 (2.5145%) in 22918 runs

measured displacement per target section:
   .data     +20480 (0x5000)  agreed by 15932 of 15932 references (100.0%)
   .rdata     +1040 (0x410)   agreed by  6049 of  6986 references ( 86.6%)

references agreeing with their section's displacement : 21981
IAT slots resolving into our own import table         :   937
UNEXPLAINED                                           :     0
```

The `.rdata` figure is not a shortfall: the 937 that disagree are `call dword ptr [__imp__X]`, which
name an import slot. The linker builds its own import table, so ours sits at a different offset and
holds the slots in a different order - the delta is per-symbol, and no single number describes it.
Those are checked by landing inside our import table instead.

That `.rdata`'s *contents* move `+0x410` while its header does not is the same effect, and it is why
the displacement is measured rather than read from the section headers. Assuming the header value
would flag 6,049 correct references as corruption.

## Three more defects, all found only by comparing every byte

The assembler and linker were happy through all three. Layout checks were happy. Only a
byte-for-byte comparison that asks *where each difference points* found them.

**A raw name in `nearest()`.** `sortedSymbols` is filled from `symbolAt` inside `buildSymbolIndex`,
which runs *before* `assignUniqueNames`, so its values are Ghidra's raw names. `nearest()` returned
one, emitting `offset RTTI_Type_Descriptor + 0` for a reference to `0x578C84`; the linker resolved it
to the first symbol of that name, 48 KB away. 144 sites in `.text` held that one address, and across
the image **387 built addresses were being reached from more than one retail address**, one of them
from 48 distinct sources.

**Disambiguation that depended on sort order.** When several addresses share a name, the first in
ascending order kept the plain name. That is not always the address the name belongs to: Ghidra had
`FUN_0053b910` at `0x53B570` *and* at `0x53B910`, so the plain name went to the wrong one. A
colliding name is now suffixed at *every* address, including the first, which removes the ordering
dependence entirely. 91 names collide here.

**An entry point that is not the lowest address of its body.** Two separate cases, and the exporter
assumed neither could happen:

- `FUN_0053b910`'s body is non-contiguous - a five-byte fragment at `0x53B570` plus the real body at
  `0x53B910`. The walk met the fragment first and named it, so all **32 calls** to that function
  landed `0x3A0` short, in five bytes that are not a function. The PROC name is now keyed on the
  entry point, not on which piece the walk reaches first.
- `caseD_0`'s body starts at `0x4EBDD5` but its entry is `0x4EBDE5`, sixteen bytes *later*. The PROC
  opens at the body start, so by the time the walk reaches the entry it is inside an open procedure
  and defines nothing there. The entry now gets a label whenever the open PROC was not opened at it.

The second of those only became visible *because* the first was fixed - it turned from a jump table
silently pointing 16 bytes low into an honest `A2006: undefined symbol`. That is the argument for
fixing the loud failures first.

## The assembly is readable, and that changed no bytes

`tools/verify_encodings.py` settles which mnemonics MASM reproduces exactly, by measurement rather
than by reasoning about encoding tables: it recovers every distinct encoding in the binary, assembles
each in its own 16-byte `ALIGN` slot with the real `ml.exe`, and keeps only those that come back
byte-identical. The hazard it exists for is narrow and sharp - x86 has redundant encodings, so a
correctly-spelled mnemonic can assemble to different bytes of a *different length*, which moves every
address after it and breaks the layout guarantee, silently.

Over 363,788 testable instruction occurrences in 36,241 unique forms:

| | count | share |
|---|--:|--:|
| emit as mnemonic (byte-identical) | 362,477 | **99.64%** |
| must stay `db` (MASM re-encodes) | 1,302 | 0.36% |
| must stay `db` (syntax unsupported) | 9 | 0.00% |

The 1,302 are genuine ambiguities of exactly the kind the tool was built to catch: `fadd st, st` is
`DC C0` in retail but `D8 C0` from MASM; `mov dword ptr [esp], eax` is `89 44 24 00` but MASM emits
the shorter `89 04 24`; `test bl, ah` and its operand-swapped twin differ only in the direction bit.
Those keep their bytes forever, and the fallback is per *instruction*, not per file.

`operator new` now reads as itself:

```asm
??2@YAPAXI@Z PROC
    push 1
    push dword ptr [esp + 8]
    db 0E8h
    dd __nh_malloc - ($ + 4)
    pop ecx
    pop ecx
    ret
??2@YAPAXI@Z ENDP
```

**Crucially, `verify_text.py` still reports 0 unexplained after the switch.** That is the proof the
table is doing its job: 362,135 instructions changed from raw bytes to text and not one byte of the
linked `.text` moved.

## Retail layout: every section byte-identical, and it boots

The link used to build its own import table at the front of `.rdata`, which pushed `.data` 0x5000
high. Code references into `.data` were relocated, but any pointer stored in data that Ghidra had
not flagged kept its retail value and pointed 0x5000 low. The first boot crashed on exactly such a
stale string pointer, 11 seconds in.

That whole class of problem is now gone, rather than patched one pointer at a time:

- **No import libraries.** Retail's import table is part of `.rdata`, so the exporter emits it as
  data, with each IAT slot labelled `__imp__Name` (raw bytes, since the slots hold RVAs or
  ordinals). Every import is called through its retail slot. With nothing to import, the linker
  builds no table of its own.
- **Sections end at their virtual size.** Ghidra's blocks run on through the file padding, and the
  linker's few bytes of its own (debug directory, volatile metadata) then pushed `.data` a page
  high. `trimToSection` cuts each block at retail's virtual size and refuses if anything is
  addressed in the padding.
- **`.rsrc` is its own section**, so it lands at its retail address and its directory, which holds
  RVAs, works as it stands.
- **`tools/link.py` adopts retail's import, IAT and resource directories** into the header, and
  refuses the build if any section is not at its retail address.

Result: `.text`, `.rdata`, `.data` and `.rsrc` all match retail byte for byte, 0 differing bytes in
2.7 MB. Launched from the game folder, `OpenMCM2.exe` plays the intro, reaches the menus and runs
a race.

## Porting a function to C

A C function marked `PORT(0x004890B0)` on the line before it replaces the game's function at that
address. `tools/port.py` does the work in the assembly, between export and assembling:

- the original is renamed `orig_<name>`, its bytes left where they were, so `.text` keeps its
  layout and the original stays callable from C as `game_orig_<name>`;
- every call and jump to it now goes to the C function. The exporter writes those as opcode bytes
  and `dd name - ($ + 4)`, which MASM refuses for an external target, so they are rewritten as the
  `call`, `jmp` or conditional jump they encode, which assembles to the same five or six bytes;
- a port is refused if code elsewhere jumps to a label inside the function, since that code would
  go on running half of the original under the port.

The calling convention has to be the original's exactly. A `__thiscall` method is written as
`__fastcall` with an unused second parameter, which takes EDX.

Win32 calls in ported code go through the game's own import slots, and anything the game does not
import is looked up at run time, so no import table is built and `.rdata` does not grow.

`tools/verify.py` then compares every section with retail. The only differences it accepts are
4-byte references whose retail target is a ported function and whose built target is in `.open`;
anything else fails the build. That proves the game's code is untouched and the redirection is
complete. It proves nothing about the port itself, so each one is also checked against the
original's instructions (disassembling the linked function) and in game.

## Still to do

- **Checks on what ported code does.** A port is compared with the original by reading both; a
  test that runs the two side by side on the same input, through `game_orig_<name>`, is owed.

```

`tools/coff.py`:

```py
# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
"""Just enough of the COFF object format for the build: sections and the symbol table."""
import struct

SECTION_HEADER = 40
SYMBOL = 18

UNINITIALIZED = 0x00000080
INITIALIZED = 0x00000040
KEEP = 0x00F00000 | 0x00001000 | 0x01000000     # alignment, COMDAT, relocation overflow
READ_WRITE_EXECUTE = 0xE0000000


def header(data):
    """(section count, symbol table offset, symbol count) of an object file."""
    machine, sections, _, symtab, symbols, opt_size, _ = struct.unpack_from("<HHIIIHH", data, 0)
    if machine != 0x14C or opt_size != 0:
        raise ValueError("not an x86 object file")
    return sections, symtab, symbols


def symbol_name(data, offset, strings):
    raw = data[offset:offset + 8]
    if raw[:4] == b"\0\0\0\0":
        start = strings + struct.unpack_from("<I", raw, 4)[0]
        return data[start:data.index(b"\0", start)].decode()
    return raw.rstrip(b"\0").decode()


def symbols(data):
    """(name, section number, storage class) for every symbol; section 0 means undefined."""
    _, symtab, count = header(data)
    strings = symtab + count * SYMBOL
    i = 0
    while i < count:
        offset = symtab + i * SYMBOL
        section, = struct.unpack_from("<h", data, offset + 12)
        storage, aux = data[offset + 16], data[offset + 17]
        yield symbol_name(data, offset, strings), section, storage
        i += 1 + aux


def move_sections(data, rename):
    """Rename sections, making each renamed one initialised, readable, writable and executable.

    `rename` maps a section name to its new one, or returns it unchanged to leave the section
    alone. A zero-filled section gets real zero bytes appended to the file, since the linker
    places zero-filled data apart from initialised data. New names must fit the eight bytes of
    the section header.
    """
    out = bytearray(data)
    tail = bytearray()
    for i in range(header(data)[0]):
        offset = 20 + i * SECTION_HEADER
        name = out[offset:offset + 8].rstrip(b"\0").decode()
        new = rename(name)
        if new == name:
            continue
        if len(new) > 8:
            raise ValueError("section name too long: " + new)

        size, pointer = struct.unpack_from("<II", out, offset + 16)
        flags, = struct.unpack_from("<I", out, offset + 36)
        if flags & UNINITIALIZED:
            pointer = len(data) + len(tail)
            tail += bytes(size)
            struct.pack_into("<I", out, offset + 20, pointer)

        out[offset:offset + 8] = new.encode().ljust(8, b"\0")
        struct.pack_into("<I", out, offset + 36, (flags & KEEP) | INITIALIZED | READ_WRITE_EXECUTE)
    return bytes(out + tail)

```

`tools/compile.py`:

```py
# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
"""Compile code/mcm2/*.c into build/obj for the link.

The objects are built without the CRT (the game carries its own, and its startup runs, not the
modern one). Their sections are then moved into one section of our own, .open, so that none of
our code, constants or data lands in the game's .text, .rdata or .data: those three must keep
their retail sizes and addresses.

Every part of .open is marked as initialised data that can also be executed. The linker places
sections by kind, and only initialised data goes after the game's .data: a code section would go
straight after .text, and zero-filled data before .rdata, moving every section the game addresses.
"""
import glob
import os
import shutil
import subprocess
import sys

import coff

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
ROOT = os.path.dirname(HERE)
SRC = os.path.join(ROOT, "code", "mcm2")
OBJ = os.path.join(ROOT, "build", "obj")

FLAGS = ["/nologo", "/c", "/O2", "/W4", "/WX", "/GS-", "/Zl", "/Gy", "/std:c11", "/D_CRT_SECURE_NO_WARNINGS"]

# Where each kind of section goes within .open. Section names are limited to eight characters,
# so the compiler's own $ suffixes (.text$mn) are dropped.
SECTIONS = {".text": ".open$t", ".rdata": ".open$r", ".data": ".open$d", ".bss": ".open$z"}

# Sections whose contents need the modern CRT's startup, which never runs here.
UNSUPPORTED = (".CRT", ".tls")


def msvc_environment():
    """The environment vcvarsall sets up for 32-bit builds."""
    vswhere = os.path.join(os.environ["ProgramFiles(x86)"], "Microsoft Visual Studio", "Installer", "vswhere.exe")
    vs = subprocess.run([vswhere, "-latest", "-property", "installationPath"],
                        capture_output=True, text=True, check=True).stdout.strip()
    vcvars = os.path.join(vs, "VC", "Auxiliary", "Build", "vcvarsall.bat")
    out = subprocess.run('"%s" x86 >nul && set' % vcvars, shell=True, capture_output=True, text=True, check=True).stdout
    return dict(line.split("=", 1) for line in out.splitlines() if "=" in line)


def rename(name):
    base = name.partition("$")[0]
    if base in UNSUPPORTED:
        raise ValueError("section %s needs CRT startup, which does not run in this image" % name)
    return SECTIONS.get(base, name)


def main():
    sources = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(SRC, "*.c")))
    os.makedirs(OBJ, exist_ok=True)
    for stale in glob.glob(os.path.join(OBJ, "*.obj")):
        os.remove(stale)
    if not sources:
        print("no sources in %s" % SRC)
        return

    env = msvc_environment()
    cl = shutil.which("cl", path=env.get("Path") or env.get("PATH"))
    res = subprocess.run([cl] + FLAGS + ["/Fo" + OBJ + "\\"] + sources, env=env, cwd=SRC,
                         capture_output=True, text=True)
    print((res.stdout + res.stderr).strip())
    if res.returncode != 0:
        sys.exit("compile failed")

    for path in glob.glob(os.path.join(OBJ, "*.obj")):
        with open(path, "rb") as f:
            data = f.read()
        with open(path, "wb") as f:
            f.write(coff.move_sections(data, rename))
    print("compiled %d file(s) -> %s" % (len(sources), OBJ))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

```

`tools/encodings_table.py`:

```py
# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
"""The verified instruction-text table, shared by everything that has to measure game.asm.

ExportAsm.java used to emit .text as nothing but `db` runs, so any tool that needed the byte
length of a line could count commas. It now emits a real mnemonic wherever one assembles back to
retail's exact bytes (see tools/verify_encodings.py), and a mnemonic line is opaque to that count.

Getting the length wrong is not a visible failure, which is why this lives in one place instead of
being re-derived per tool:

  - tools/asm.py pads a ported function with exactly the bytes it removed. Undercount, and .text
    shrinks and every later address moves - breaking -FIXED -BASE:0x400000 silently.
  - tools/patches.py locates a patch by walking .text and accumulating lengths. Undercount, and
    the patch lands on the wrong bytes, or is refused as "not inside the emitted .text".

The table is the same file that drove the emission, so the lengths agree with what was emitted by
construction, rather than by a second disassembler having to independently be right.
"""
import os
import re

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
ROOT = os.path.dirname(HERE)
TABLE = os.path.join(ROOT, "data", "encodings.tsv")

# `jmp eax` is two bytes, not five, and a bare register name matches the symbol pattern used
# for the rel32 rule below. Register-operand branches are in the encoding table; this list
# only stops one from reaching the rel32 rule if the table is ever missing an entry.
REGISTERS = frozenset("""eax ebx ecx edx esi edi ebp esp
                         ax bx cx dx si di bp sp
                         al bl cl dl ah bh ch dh""".split())

_cache = None


def load(path=None):
    """MASM text -> the bytes it assembles to. Empty when no table has been generated yet."""
    global _cache
    if path is None and _cache is not None:
        return _cache

    table = {}
    src = path or TABLE
    if os.path.exists(src):
        collisions = set()
        with open(src, encoding="utf-8") as f:
            for line in f:
                if not line or line.startswith("#"):
                    continue
                hexbytes, _, text = line.rstrip("\n").partition("\t")
                if not text:
                    continue
                raw = bytes.fromhex(hexbytes)
                if text in table and table[text] != raw:
                    # Two encodings rendering to one mnemonic would make the text ambiguous and its
                    # length unknowable. It should not happen - only byte-identical round-trips are
                    # in this table - but guessing would silently misplace everything after it.
                    collisions.add(text)
                table[text] = raw
        for text in collisions:
            del table[text]

    if path is None:
        _cache = table
    return table


def line_length(stripped, table):
    """Byte length of one emitted .text line, or None if it does not contribute bytes.

    Returns 0 for the directives that occupy no space (labels, PROC/ENDP, PUBLIC, comments) and
    None for a line that looks like an instruction but cannot be measured - the caller must treat
    that as a hard error rather than as zero.
    """
    s = stripped
    if not s or s.startswith(";"):
        return 0
    # A trailing comment contributes no bytes. tools/asm_vtables.py annotates the lines it rewrites
    # ("__vft_orig_005B524C LABEL BYTE   ; was ??_7mmCityInfo@@6B@..."), and everything below
    # matches on the end of the line. Safe to cut at the first semicolon because ExportAsm.java
    # emits data only as `db 0XXh` runs - there are no quoted strings for one to hide in.
    cut = s.find(";")
    if cut > 0:
        s = s[:cut].strip()
        if not s:
            return 0
    if s.startswith("db "):
        # tools/asm.py pads a stripped function with `db <n> dup (0CCh)`, which is n bytes, not the
        # one byte a comma count would give.
        m = re.match(r"db\s+(\d+)\s+dup\s*\(", s)
        if m:
            return int(m.group(1))
        return s[3:].count(",") + 1
    if s.startswith("dd "):
        return 4
    if s.startswith("dw "):
        return 2
    if s.startswith("dq "):
        return 8
    # `D_5B0000 LABEL BYTE` names an address without occupying one. These only appear in the data
    # sections, which is why they surfaced the first time tools/asm_vtables.py - the only consumer
    # that walks .rdata - started measuring through here.
    if re.match(r"^\S+\s+LABEL\s+\w+\s*$", s):
        return 0
    if s in table:
        return len(table[s])
    # tools/asm.py rewrites `db 0E8h` + `dd SYM - ($ + 4)` into `call SYM` once SYM is EXTERN,
    # because MASM cannot evaluate the subtraction across modules. An external target's distance is
    # unknown at assembly time, so MASM has to emit the rel32 form: five bytes for both call and
    # jmp. The indirect forms never reach here - they are in the table above.
    # A symbol operand only. An indirect `call dword ptr [ecx + 54h]` also starts with a
    # letter, and answering 5 for its 3 bytes would silently shift everything after it -
    # so anything with a memory operand falls through to the hard error below instead.
    m = re.match(r"^(?:call|jmp)\s+([?_@$A-Za-z][\w?@$]*)\s*$", s)
    if m and m.group(1).lower() not in REGISTERS:
        return 5
    if (s.endswith(":") or s.startswith("PUBLIC") or " PROC" in s or " ENDP" in s
            or s.startswith("ALIGN") or s.startswith("INCLUDE") or s.startswith("OPTION")
            or s.startswith("ASSUME") or s.startswith("EXTERN") or s.startswith("EXTRN")
            or s.startswith("END") or s.startswith(".")):
        return 0
    return None

```

`tools/ghidra/Attribute.java`:

```java
// Attribute every function to the .cpp it was compiled from.
//
// Mcm2.exe carries 115 of its own source paths - `D:\aardvark\VC\krusty2\PCVideoCard.cpp` and so on
// - left behind by assert macros. Each one is referenced from the functions that live in that file,
// so the strings are a free module map: they turn a wall of FUN_004a1bf0 into "this is in
// PCVideoCard.cpp", which is the difference between searching 5,658 functions and searching twenty.
//
// Attribution spreads two ways, and both are needed:
//   DIRECT   - the function itself references the path, because it contains an assert.
//   CALLERS  - most functions contain no assert at all. But an assert helper is called from within
//              one file far more often than across files, so a function with no path of its own is
//              given the module its NEIGHBOURS agree on, by majority of the functions it calls and
//              is called by. This is a heuristic and is reported separately from the direct hits.
//
// Writes modules.txt (one line per function) and a per-module summary.
//
// @category OpenMCM2

import java.io.File;
import java.io.PrintWriter;
import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.Collections;
import java.util.HashMap;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.Map;
import java.util.TreeMap;

import ghidra.app.script.GhidraScript;
import ghidra.program.model.address.Address;
import ghidra.program.model.data.StringDataInstance;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.Data;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.DataIterator;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.Function;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.FunctionIterator;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.Instruction;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.Listing;
import ghidra.program.model.mem.MemoryBlock;
import ghidra.program.model.symbol.Reference;

public class Attribute extends GhidraScript {

    static String baseName(String p) {
        int i = Math.max(p.lastIndexOf('\\'), p.lastIndexOf('/'));
        return i >= 0 ? p.substring(i + 1) : p;
    }

    @Override
    public void run() throws Exception {
        String outDir = "C:/mcm2ghidra/out";
        String[] args = getScriptArgs();
        if (args.length > 0 && args[0].length() > 0) {
            outDir = args[0];
        }
        new File(outDir).mkdirs();
        Listing listing = currentProgram.getListing();

        // ---- find the source-path strings, by scanning bytes rather than trusting the listing ----
        // Ghidra has not necessarily typed these as strings, and a path that was never typed would
        // simply be invisible to getDefinedData.
        Map<Long, String> pathAt = new HashMap<>();
        for (MemoryBlock b : currentProgram.getMemory().getBlocks()) {
            if (!b.isInitialized() || b.isExecute()) {
                continue;
            }
            byte[] buf = new byte[(int) b.getSize()];
            b.getBytes(b.getStart(), buf);
            long base = b.getStart().getOffset();
            for (int i = 0; i + 8 < buf.length; i++) {
                if (buf[i] != 'a' || buf[i + 1] != 'a' || buf[i + 2] != 'r' || buf[i + 3] != 'd') {
                    continue;
                }
                if (!(buf[i + 4] == 'v' && buf[i + 5] == 'a' && buf[i + 6] == 'r' && buf[i + 7] == 'k')) {
                    continue;
                }
                int s = i;
                while (s > 0 && buf[s - 1] >= 32 && buf[s - 1] < 127 && i - s < 80) {
                    s--;
                }
                int e = i;
                while (e < buf.length && buf[e] >= 32 && buf[e] < 127 && e - i < 80) {
                    e++;
                }
                String p = new String(buf, s, e - s, "ASCII");
                if (p.toLowerCase().endsWith(".cpp") || p.toLowerCase().endsWith(".c")
                        || p.toLowerCase().endsWith(".h")) {
                    pathAt.put(base + s, baseName(p));
                }
                i = e;
            }
        }
        println("source-path strings found: " + pathAt.size());

        // ---- direct attribution: a function that references one ----
        Map<Function, String> module = new HashMap<>();
        int direct = 0;
        for (Map.Entry<Long, String> e : pathAt.entrySet()) {
            Address a = toAddr(e.getKey());
            for (Reference r : getReferencesTo(a)) {
                Function f = getFunctionContaining(r.getFromAddress());
                if (f == null) {
                    continue;
                }
                // A function can reference more than one path only if the compiler inlined across
                // files; first one wins and the count below shows how rare it is.
                if (module.putIfAbsent(f, e.getValue()) == null) {
                    direct++;
                }
            }
        }
        println("functions attributed DIRECTLY: " + direct);

        // ---- spread to neighbours, by majority vote, until it stops changing ----
        List<Function> all = new ArrayList<>();
        FunctionIterator it = currentProgram.getFunctionManager().getFunctions(true);
        while (it.hasNext()) {
            Function f = it.next();
            if (!f.isExternal()) {
                all.add(f);
            }
        }
        int spread = 0;
        for (int pass = 0; pass < 6; pass++) {
            int changed = 0;
            for (Function f : all) {
                if (module.containsKey(f)) {
                    continue;
                }
                Map<String, Integer> votes = new HashMap<>();
                for (Function c : f.getCalledFunctions(monitor)) {
                    String m = module.get(c);
                    if (m != null) {
                        votes.merge(m, 1, Integer::sum);
                    }
                }
                for (Function c : f.getCallingFunctions(monitor)) {
                    String m = module.get(c);
                    if (m != null) {
                        votes.merge(m, 2, Integer::sum);   // a caller is the stronger signal
                    }
                }
                String best = null;
                int bestN = 0;
                for (Map.Entry<String, Integer> v : votes.entrySet()) {
                    if (v.getValue() > bestN) {
                        bestN = v.getValue();
                        best = v.getKey();
                    }
                }
                if (best != null && bestN >= 2) {
                    module.put(f, best);
                    changed++;
                }
            }
            spread += changed;
            println("  pass " + (pass + 1) + ": +" + changed);
            if (changed == 0) {
                break;
            }
        }

        // ---- report ----
        TreeMap<String, List<Function>> byModule = new TreeMap<>();
        for (Map.Entry<Function, String> e : module.entrySet()) {
            byModule.computeIfAbsent(e.getValue(), k -> new ArrayList<>()).add(e.getKey());
        }
        println("");
        println("functions attributed: " + module.size() + " of " + all.size()
                + " (" + direct + " directly, " + spread + " by neighbours)");
        println("modules with functions: " + byModule.size());

        PrintWriter w = new PrintWriter(new File(outDir, "modules.txt"), "UTF-8");
        w.println("# function address, module, name");
        for (Map.Entry<String, List<Function>> e : byModule.entrySet()) {
            List<Function> fs = e.getValue();
            fs.sort((x, y) -> x.getEntryPoint().compareTo(y.getEntryPoint()));
            for (Function f : fs) {
                w.printf("0x%08X %-34s %s%n", f.getEntryPoint().getOffset(), e.getKey(), f.getName());
            }
        }
        w.close();

        // The modules this session actually needs, printed so they can be read without the file.
        String[] interesting = {"PCVideoCard.cpp", "VideoCard.cpp", "PCRenderTarget.cpp",
                                "OptionProcs.cpp", "dlgprocs.cpp", "gameui.cpp", "krustyui.cpp"};
        println("");
        for (String m : interesting) {
            List<Function> fs = byModule.get(m);
            if (fs == null) {
                println(m + ": none");
                continue;
            }
            fs.sort((x, y) -> x.getEntryPoint().compareTo(y.getEntryPoint()));
            StringBuilder sb = new StringBuilder();
            for (int i = 0; i < Math.min(fs.size(), 14); i++) {
                sb.append(String.format("0x%08X ", fs.get(i).getEntryPoint().getOffset()));
            }
            println(String.format("%-20s %3d functions: %s", m, fs.size(), sb.toString()));
        }
        println("");
        println("wrote " + new File(outDir, "modules.txt"));
    }
}

```

`tools/ghidra/Census.java`:

```java
// What the freshly imported database actually contains.
//
// This is the baseline every later claim is measured against, and it exists because "Ghidra
// analysed it" is not a fact about coverage. The number that matters most for the assemble-and-link
// harness is the LAST one: bytes of .text that no function body covers. Those bytes still have to
// be emitted, in order, or every address after them moves - they are jump tables, alignment
// padding, orphaned stubs and any code the analyser did not find. A large or growing figure there
// is the single best early warning that the export will not reproduce retail's layout.
//
// Writes census.txt next to the other output so it can be diffed after a re-import.
//
// @category OpenMCM2

import java.io.File;
import java.io.PrintWriter;
import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.HashMap;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.Map;
import java.util.TreeMap;

import ghidra.app.script.GhidraScript;
import ghidra.program.model.address.Address;
import ghidra.program.model.address.AddressSetView;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.CodeUnit;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.Data;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.Function;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.FunctionIterator;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.Instruction;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.InstructionIterator;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.Listing;
import ghidra.program.model.mem.MemoryBlock;
import ghidra.program.model.symbol.Symbol;
import ghidra.program.model.symbol.SymbolIterator;
import ghidra.program.model.symbol.SymbolTable;

public class Census extends GhidraScript {

    StringBuilder out = new StringBuilder();

    void say(String s) {
        println(s);
        out.append(s).append('\n');
    }

    @Override
    public void run() throws Exception {
        String outDir = "C:/mcm2ghidra/out";
        String[] args = getScriptArgs();
        if (args.length > 0 && args[0].length() > 0) {
            outDir = args[0];
        }
        new File(outDir).mkdirs();

        say("program   : " + currentProgram.getName());
        say("format    : " + currentProgram.getExecutableFormat());
        say("compiler  : " + currentProgram.getCompilerSpec().getCompilerSpecID());
        say("image base: " + currentProgram.getImageBase());
        say("md5       : " + currentProgram.getExecutableMD5());
        say("sha256    : " + currentProgram.getExecutableSHA256());
        say("");

        say("sections:");
        for (MemoryBlock b : currentProgram.getMemory().getBlocks()) {
            say(String.format("   %-10s %s .. %s  %8d bytes  %s%s%s%s",
                    b.getName(), b.getStart(), b.getEnd(), b.getSize(),
                    b.isRead() ? "r" : "-", b.isWrite() ? "w" : "-",
                    b.isExecute() ? "x" : "-", b.isInitialized() ? "" : "  UNINITIALISED"));
        }
        say("");

        Listing listing = currentProgram.getListing();

        // ---- functions ----
        long nfunc = 0, ndefault = 0, nthunk = 0, nexternal = 0;
        long bodyBytes = 0;
        FunctionIterator fit = listing.getFunctions(true);
        while (fit.hasNext()) {
            Function f = fit.next();
            if (f.isExternal()) {
                nexternal++;
                continue;
            }
            nfunc++;
            if (f.isThunk()) {
                nthunk++;
            }
            // Ghidra names an undiscovered-purpose function FUN_<addr>. The ratio of those to named
            // ones says how much symbol recovery is still owed, and on a binary with no map file
            // and no exports it starts near 100%.
            if (f.getName().startsWith("FUN_")) {
                ndefault++;
            }
            bodyBytes += f.getBody().getNumAddresses();
        }
        say(String.format("functions          : %d  (%d thunks, %d external)", nfunc, nthunk, nexternal));
        say(String.format("  default-named    : %d  (%.1f%% still FUN_*)",
                ndefault, nfunc == 0 ? 0.0 : 100.0 * ndefault / nfunc));
        say(String.format("  bytes in bodies  : %d", bodyBytes));

        // ---- instructions and data ----
        long ninstr = 0;
        InstructionIterator iit = listing.getInstructions(true);
        while (iit.hasNext()) {
            iit.next();
            ninstr++;
        }
        long ndata = 0, ndefined = 0;
        for (Data d : listing.getDefinedData(true)) {
            ndata++;
            if (!d.getDataType().getName().equals("undefined")) {
                ndefined++;
            }
        }
        say(String.format("instructions       : %d", ninstr));
        say(String.format("defined data items : %d  (%d with a real type)", ndata, ndefined));

        // ---- symbols ----
        SymbolTable st = currentProgram.getSymbolTable();
        long nsym = 0, nprimary = 0;
        SymbolIterator sit = st.getAllSymbols(true);
        while (sit.hasNext()) {
            Symbol s = sit.next();
            nsym++;
            if (s.isPrimary()) {
                nprimary++;
            }
        }
        say(String.format("symbols            : %d  (%d primary)", nsym, nprimary));
        say("");

        // ---- the number that governs the harness ----
        // Every byte of .text has to be emitted in address order. Bytes no function covers are
        // still real content - jump tables, padding, stubs - and if the exporter drops or pads them
        // the section changes length and, with no relocation table, every absolute address baked
        // into the code afterwards points somewhere wrong.
        MemoryBlock text = currentProgram.getMemory().getBlock(".text");
        if (text != null) {
            long total = text.getSize();
            long covered = 0;
            long gaps = 0;
            Address a = text.getStart();
            Address end = text.getEnd();
            boolean inGap = false;
            long gapBytes = 0;
            List<long[]> biggest = new ArrayList<>();
            long gapStart = 0;
            while (a != null && a.compareTo(end) <= 0) {
                Function f = listing.getFunctionContaining(a);
                if (f != null) {
                    if (inGap) {
                        biggest.add(new long[]{gapStart, a.getOffset() - gapStart});
                        inGap = false;
                    }
                    long len = f.getBody().getMaxAddress().getOffset() - a.getOffset() + 1;
                    covered += len;
                    a = a.add(len);
                    continue;
                }
                if (!inGap) {
                    inGap = true;
                    gapStart = a.getOffset();
                    gaps++;
                }
                gapBytes++;
                if (a.equals(end)) {
                    break;
                }
                a = a.add(1);
            }
            if (inGap) {
                biggest.add(new long[]{gapStart, end.getOffset() - gapStart + 1});
            }
            say(String.format(".text              : %d bytes", total));
            say(String.format("  covered by funcs : %d  (%.2f%%)", covered, 100.0 * covered / total));
            say(String.format("  NOT covered      : %d in %d runs  (%.2f%%)",
                    gapBytes, gaps, 100.0 * gapBytes / total));
            biggest.sort((x, y) -> Long.compare(y[1], x[1]));
            say("  largest uncovered runs (these must still be emitted, in order):");
            for (int i = 0; i < Math.min(12, biggest.size()); i++) {
                say(String.format("     0x%08X  %6d bytes", biggest.get(i)[0], biggest.get(i)[1]));
            }
        }

        PrintWriter w = new PrintWriter(new File(outDir, "census.txt"), "UTF-8");
        w.print(out);
        w.close();
        say("");
        say("wrote " + new File(outDir, "census.txt"));
    }
}

```

`tools/ghidra/Decomp.java`:

```java
// Decompile the functions that reference a given string, or a list of addresses.
//
// The fastest way into unfamiliar code in a binary with no symbols is a string it prints. This
// takes a substring, finds every literal containing it, and decompiles whatever references it -
// which is usually the exact function that does the thing the string describes.
//
// Usage:  -postScript Decomp.java <outDir> str:FullScreen
//         -postScript Decomp.java <outDir> 0x004CDBF0 0x004CDCA0
//
// @category OpenMCM2

import java.io.File;
import java.io.PrintWriter;
import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.LinkedHashSet;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.Set;

import ghidra.app.decompiler.DecompInterface;
import ghidra.app.decompiler.DecompileResults;
import ghidra.app.script.GhidraScript;
import ghidra.program.model.address.Address;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.Function;
import ghidra.program.model.mem.MemoryBlock;
import ghidra.program.model.symbol.Reference;

public class Decomp extends GhidraScript {

    @Override
    public void run() throws Exception {
        String[] args = getScriptArgs();
        String outDir = args.length > 0 ? args[0] : "C:/mcm2ghidra/out";
        new File(outDir).mkdirs();

        Set<Function> targets = new LinkedHashSet<>();

        for (int i = 1; i < args.length; i++) {
            String a = args[i];
            if (a.startsWith("str:")) {
                String needle = a.substring(4);
                byte[] pat = needle.getBytes("ASCII");
                for (MemoryBlock b : currentProgram.getMemory().getBlocks()) {
                    if (!b.isInitialized() || b.isExecute()) {
                        continue;
                    }
                    byte[] buf = new byte[(int) b.getSize()];
                    b.getBytes(b.getStart(), buf);
                    long base = b.getStart().getOffset();
                    for (int p = 0; p + pat.length <= buf.length; p++) {
                        boolean hit = true;
                        for (int k = 0; k < pat.length; k++) {
                            if (buf[p + k] != pat[k]) {
                                hit = false;
                                break;
                            }
                        }
                        if (!hit) {
                            continue;
                        }
                        // back up to the start of the literal so the reference is found
                        int s = p;
                        while (s > 0 && buf[s - 1] >= 32 && buf[s - 1] < 127 && p - s < 120) {
                            s--;
                        }
                        Address at = toAddr(base + s);
                        for (Reference r : getReferencesTo(at)) {
                            Function f = getFunctionContaining(r.getFromAddress());
                            if (f != null) {
                                println("string \"" + needle + "\" at " + at + " referenced by "
                                        + f.getName() + " @ " + f.getEntryPoint());
