```java
     * reference and no neighbours has no evidence at all, and is left as the bytes it already is.
     */
    /**
     * Do these bytes look like an entry in a string table?
     *
     * Two earlier guards failed on the same address. Ghidra's string typing failed because it had
     * never typed "texture\0tga\0" as a string. Requiring a strided run of pointers failed because
     * this dword did not come through the exact-symbol rule at all - Ghidra had recorded a
     * reference to the location, so the emitter trusted it and asked nearest() for a name, getting
     * "?PLAYER@@3PAVmmPlayer@@A + 219700". A pointer 214 KB past the nearest symbol is not a
     * pointer; it is four bytes of ASCII that happen to be numerically plausible.
     *
     * So this decides from the bytes themselves, needing no analysis to have been done. A string
     * table entry is printable characters ending in a terminator, immediately preceded by another
     * C string. For a real pointer to be rejected here it would have to have all three low bytes
     * printable, a zero high byte, and sit directly after a NUL-terminated string.
     */
    /** A printable, letter-bearing run starting at `from` and walking in `step`, past any padding. */
    boolean plausibleDataAddr(long v) {
        if (v < 0x400000L) {
            return false;
        }
        MemoryBlock b = currentProgram.getMemory().getBlock(toAddr(v));
        return b != null && EMITTED_DATA.contains(b.getName());
    }

    /**
     * How long an ascending table of data pointers this address sits in.
     *
     * The text heuristics were needed because a dword of ASCII looks like an address. This is the
     * same ambiguity seen from the other side: vehLoadNameList is an array of 128 char*, and the
     * entries whose low byte happened to be a letter - 0x005D4864 is 'd','H',']' - were classified
     * as text and left unrelocated while their neighbours relocated correctly. Half a pointer array
     * is worse than none: mmVehList::LoadAll read one, called SetDefaultVehicle with a retail
     * address that means nothing in our image, and the game died on the first vehicle it loaded.
     *
     * What tells the two apart is order. A table of string pointers ascends, in small steps,
     * because it points at consecutive strings - vehLoadNameList climbs by 4, 8 and 12 bytes at a
     * time. Four bytes of ASCII carry no such relationship to the four bytes after them, so a run
     * of four consecutive ascending entries does not happen by accident.
     *
     * This overrides the text heuristics rather than being overridden by them: ascending order is
     * positive evidence about structure, while "these bytes could be read as letters" is an
     * observation about coincidence.
     */
    /**
     * Is this dword one of several pointers inside the same named object?
     *
     * The run rules reason about a window of neighbours and cannot see an object's boundaries, so a
     * short table defeats them: `lpOutputString` is four entries, and no threshold that keeps it
     * also keeps four bytes of ASCII out. Raising and lowering that threshold traded one defect for
     * the other for several rounds.
     *
     * A recovered symbol name is the boundary the run rules were missing. `lpOutputString` is a
     * named object, small, and most of its dwords address the emitted data sections - which is what
     * an array of pointers looks like and what a string does not. The size cap is what keeps a
     * string blob out: those are named too, but they are long, and a handful of coincidental
     * addresses among hundreds of bytes is not evidence of anything.
     *
     * This matters more than its size suggests. `lpOutputString` is the message-prefix array behind
     * every Displayf, Warningf and Errorf in the game, so two stale entries in it hand the printer
     * an unbounded string - harmless while they point into zeroed BSS, and a stack smash the moment
     * the section layout moves.
     */
    /**
     * Objects that are known to be arrays of pointers, named explicitly.
     *
     * Six successive rules were tried to infer this and every one traded one defect for the other:
     * strict enough to keep four bytes of ASCII out, and `lpOutputString` stays broken; loose
     * enough to catch it, and real strings get rewritten as pointers. The distinguishing evidence
     * simply is not in the bytes for a four-entry table.
     *
     * So this says which objects they are. It does not generalise, and it is not meant to - it is a
     * short list of things that were established by reading the code that uses them, which is the
     * only source that actually settles the question.
     *
     * `lpOutputString` earns its place: it is the message-prefix array behind every Displayf,
     * Warningf and Errorf in the game. Two stale entries hand the printer an unbounded string,
     * which is harmless while they point into zeroed BSS and a stack smash the moment the section
     * layout moves - which is exactly what porting the next few hundred bytes of anything does.
     */
    static final Set<String> POINTER_ARRAYS = new HashSet<>(java.util.Arrays.asList(
            "lpOutputString"));

    boolean inNamedPointerObject(long a) throws Exception {
        Map.Entry<Long, String> e = sortedSymbols.floorEntry(a);
        if (e == null || !POINTER_ARRAYS.contains(e.getValue())) {
            return false;
        }

        Long next = sortedSymbols.higherKey(e.getKey());
        long objEnd = next != null ? next : e.getKey() + 0x40;
        return a + 4 <= objEnd && plausibleDataAddr(readDword(a));
    }

    /** A real recovered name, as opposed to one Ghidra invented. */
    boolean isLinkableOrNamed(String name) {
        if (name == null) {
            return false;
        }
        for (String prefix : new String[] {
                "DAT_", "LAB_", "FUN_", "SUB_", "UNK_", "unk_", "loc_", "sub_",
                "byte_", "word_", "dword_", "off_", "flt_", "dbl_", "asc_",
                "D_", "L_", "caseD_", "switchD", "default_", "nullsub_", "j_", "s_", "u_"}) {
            if (name.startsWith(prefix)) {
                return false;
            }
        }
        return true;
    }

    int pointerTableRun(long a, long start, long initEnd, boolean allowGap)
            throws Exception {
        if (!plausibleDataAddr(readDword(a))) {
            return 0;
        }

        // Monotone in EITHER direction, and at more strides than 4.
        //
        // Requiring strictly ascending stride-4 was too narrow and a static audit proved it: 21
        // pointers stayed unrelocated in four real tables. MSVC's `_rterrs` has stride 8; the
        // ".com/.exe/.bat/.cmd" extension list DESCENDS; and DefaultPrinter's message-prefix array
        // broke the run at three entries, one short of the threshold. Two of those tables point
        // into zeroed BSS today, so the wrong pointers behave correctly and nothing at runtime
        // would ever have shown it.
        //
        // Direction carries no meaning - a table of pointers to consecutive strings ascends if the
        // strings were emitted in order and descends if they were not - so requiring one direction
        // was an assumption, not evidence. What actually distinguishes a table from a coincidence
        // is that consecutive entries are *related*: same stride, same direction, small step.
        int best = 1;

        for (int stride : new int[] {4, 8, 12, 16}) {
            for (int dir = -1; dir <= 1; dir += 2) {
                int n = 1;
                int skipped = 0;

                for (long p = a; ; p += (long) dir * stride) {
                    long q = p + (long) dir * stride;
                    if (q < start || q + 4 > initEnd) {
                        break;
                    }

                    long cur = readDword(p), next = readDword(q);
                    if (!plausibleDataAddr(next)) {
                        break;
                    }

                    // Nearness, not order.
                    //
                    // Monotonicity was the third assumption in this rule to fail, and it failed on
                    // the first entry of four different tables: zlib's z_errmsg and MSVC's message
                    // arrays point at strings that were not emitted in address order, so the chain
                    // breaks at exactly one link and takes the entry with it.
                    //
                    // What actually makes a table a table is that its entries address the same
                    // neighbourhood at a fixed stride. A table of pointers to related strings does
                    // that whatever order the strings ended up in; four bytes of ASCII that happen
                    // to look like an address do not, because nothing constrains the four bytes
                    // beside them to point anywhere near.
                    long step = next - cur;
                    if (step > 4096 || step < -4096) {
                        // Tolerate one broken link, then stop.
                        //
                        // Four pointers were still being missed, every one the FIRST entry of a
                        // real table - zlib's z_errmsg, MSVC's message arrays - because the string
                        // they point at happens to sit far from the next one, so the chain snapped
                        // at exactly one link and took that entry with it. Allowing a single gap
                        // reaches across it; allowing two would start joining unrelated tables.
                        // The gap is only allowed where nothing suggests this is text. Allowing
                        // it everywhere let a run chain across a string table and rewrote two real
                        // strings as pointers - the same collision as always, one step further out.
                        if (!allowGap || skipped > 0) {
                            break;
                        }
                        skipped++;
                    }
                    n++;
                }

                if (n > best) {
                    best = n;
                }
            }
        }

        return best;
    }

    boolean stringRunNear(long from, int step) {
        try {
            long p = from;
            for (int skipped = 0; skipped < 12; skipped++) {
                if ((currentProgram.getMemory().getByte(toAddr(p)) & 0xFF) != 0) {
                    break;
                }
                p += step;
            }

            int run = 0, letters = 0;
            for (int i = 0; i < 24; i++) {
                int b = currentProgram.getMemory().getByte(toAddr(p)) & 0xFF;
                if (b < 0x20 || b >= 0x7F) {
                    break;
                }
                if ((b >= 'A' && b <= 'Z') || (b >= 'a' && b <= 'z')) {
                    letters++;
                }
                run++;
                p += step;
            }
            return run >= 3 && letters >= 2;
        } catch (Exception e) {
            return false;
        }
    }

    /** Does this run contain a printf conversion - a '%' followed by a conversion character? */
    boolean looksLikeFormat(long a, int len) {
        try {
            for (int i = 0; i + 1 < len; i++) {
                if ((currentProgram.getMemory().getByte(toAddr(a + i)) & 0xFF) != '%') {
                    continue;
                }
                for (int j = i + 1; j < len; j++) {
                    int c = currentProgram.getMemory().getByte(toAddr(a + j)) & 0xFF;
                    if ("diouxXeEfgGcspn%".indexOf(c) >= 0) {
                        return true;
                    }
                    // Flags, width and precision may sit between the % and the conversion.
                    if ("-+ #.0123456789".indexOf(c) < 0) {
                        break;
                    }
                }
            }
        } catch (Exception e) {
            return false;
        }
        return false;
    }

    boolean looksLikeStringData(long a) {
        try {
            int nul = -1;
            int letters = 0;

            for (int i = 0; i < 4; i++) {
                int b = currentProgram.getMemory().getByte(toAddr(a + i)) & 0xFF;
                if (b == 0) {
                    nul = i;
                    break;
                }
                if (b < 0x20 || b >= 0x7F) {
                    return false;
                }
                if ((b >= 'A' && b <= 'Z') || (b >= 'a' && b <= 'z')) {
                    letters++;
                }
            }

            if (nul <= 0) {
                return false;       // no characters, or no terminator inside the dword
            }

            // Two letters is what separates text from a pointer that merely prints. An address in
            // this image has its second byte in 0x40..0x6B, so one letter is common; two is not.
            // The same rule, applied independently in tools/verify_strings.py, keeps "tga" and
            // drops all sixty pointers like ",w\" that a length test alone flagged.
            // A printf conversion beats the letter count.
            //
            // Two letters is a decent proxy for "this is text", but format strings are short and
            // mostly punctuation: "%5d" has one letter, "%4d" one, "#%d" one, "%s_" one. All four
            // were relocated as pointers in a shipped build - "%5d" is 0x00643525, which lands in
            // .data, so it looked exactly like an address. The consequence is worse than a wrong
            // string: sprintf reads its arguments according to the format, so corrupting one makes
            // it misread the stack, and turning a %d into a %s hands it an integer to dereference.
            //
            // A '%' followed by a conversion character is not a coincidence in the way that three
            // printable bytes are.
            if (letters < 2 && !looksLikeFormat(a, nul)) {
                return false;
            }

            // A string keeps company with other strings; a pointer keeps company with pointers.
            // So look for a neighbouring string on EITHER side, skipping the NUL padding these
            // tables are aligned with.
            //
            // Both directions are needed. Looking only backwards missed a second copy of "tga" and
            // the extensions "bnd" and "pkg", because those sit immediately after a binary colour
            // table with no padding in front of them - but each is followed by the next entry in
            // its table ("tga\0text..."), which is just as good evidence.
            return stringRunNear(a + nul + 1, 1) || stringRunNear(a - 1, -1);
        } catch (Exception e) {
            return false;
        }
    }

    boolean inPointerRun(long a, Set<Long> candidates) {
        for (int s : new int[] {4, 8, 12, 16, 20, 24, 28, 32}) {
            int n = 1;
            if (candidates.contains(a - s)) n++;
            if (candidates.contains(a - 2L * s)) n++;
            if (candidates.contains(a + s)) n++;
            if (candidates.contains(a + 2L * s)) n++;
            if (n >= 3) {
                return true;
            }
        }
        return false;
    }

    void emitDataBlock(PrintWriter w, String blockName, String segment, String outDir)
            throws Exception {
        MemoryBlock block = currentProgram.getMemory().getBlock(blockName);
        if (block == null) {
            println("no block " + blockName);
            return;
        }

        long start = block.getStart().getOffset();
        long end = trimToSection(blockName, start, block.getEnd().getOffset() + 1);

        // Retail's import table is emitted like any other data and the image links with no
        // import libraries, so the linker builds no table of its own and every section keeps its
        // retail size and address. The descriptors and the IAT hold RVAs, which are then correct
        // as they stand; tools/link.py points the PE header at them.

        // Initialised bytes only; the rest of the block is BSS and goes to .DATA?.
        long initEnd = end;
        if (!block.isInitialized()) {
            initEnd = start;
        }

        println("emitting " + blockName + " 0x" + Long.toHexString(start)
                + "..0x" + Long.toHexString(end));

        w.println();
        w.println(segment);
        w.println();

        ReferenceManager rm = currentProgram.getReferenceManager();
        stringsProtected = 0;

        // Every 4-aligned dword whose value lands exactly on a known symbol. Collected up front so
        // a candidate can be judged by the company it keeps.
        Set<Long> candidates = new HashSet<>();
        for (long a = start; a + 4 <= initEnd; a += 4) {
            long v = readDword(a);
            if (v >= 0x400000L && symbolAt.containsKey(v)) {
                candidates.add(a);
            }
        }

        StringBuilder run = new StringBuilder();
        int runLen = 0;

        for (long a = start; a < initEnd; ) {
            String sym = nameFor(a);

            if (sym != null) {
                flushRun(w, run, runLen);
                runLen = 0;

                if (isLinkableName(sym)) {
                    w.println("PUBLIC " + sym);
                }
                w.println(sym + " LABEL BYTE");
            }

            if (neededDataLabels.contains(a)) {
                flushRun(w, run, runLen);
                runLen = 0;
                w.println(dataLabelFor(a) + " LABEL BYTE");
                dataLabelsEmitted++;
            }

            // An IAT slot: named for the code that calls through it, and kept as its raw bytes.
            // The loader overwrites it; before then it holds a hint/name RVA, or an ordinal with
            // the top bit set, and neither may be mistaken for a pointer.
            String imp = importAt.get(a);
            if (imp != null && blockName.equals(".rdata") && (a % 4) == 0) {
                flushRun(w, run, runLen);
                runLen = 0;
                w.println("PUBLIC " + imp);
                w.println(imp + " LABEL DWORD");
                w.println(String.format("    dd 0%08Xh", readDword(a)));
                a += 4;
                continue;
            }

            // Is this a pointer?
            //
            // "Value happens to fall in the image range" is not good enough - a small float is
            // 0x004xxxxx too, and symbolising one would change its value. Two things are trusted
            // instead: an exact match against a known symbol, and Ghidra's own data analysis,
            // which decides pointer-ness from the data type it recovered rather than from the
            // numeric value.
            //
            // Anything Ghidra calls a pointer but which lands INSIDE an object rather than at its
            // start is emitted as `offset SYM + delta`. That is exact, because each section is
            // written as one contiguous block, so distances within it are preserved.
            String target = null;
            long addend = 0;

            // A dword cannot be emitted as one `dd` if a label has to land INSIDE it.
            //
            // `.rdata` holds a `.text` pointer at 0x5555FC, and the code does
            // `push offset D_5555FF` - a pointer to that pointer's own last byte, which is zero.
            // That is an ordinary VC6 economy: rather than allocate an empty string, the compiler
            // points at any zero byte it already has. Emitted as `dd offset ...` there is nowhere
            // to put the label, so it was simply never written and the assembly failed with
            // `A2006: undefined symbol : D_5555FF` at the two places that use it.
            //
            // The same rule already governs `.text` - an instruction with a label inside it keeps
            // its bytes - and this is its data counterpart. Falling through emits the four bytes
            // individually, and the label lands between them.
            //
            // The cost is that the dword keeps its literal retail value instead of being
            // relocated. Here that is exactly right, because it points into `.text`, which the link
            // places at its original address. A pointer into `.rdata` or `.data` would be wrong by
            // that section's displacement, so the count is reported rather than passed over.
            boolean labelInside = neededDataLabels.contains(a + 1)
                    || neededDataLabels.contains(a + 2)
                    || neededDataLabels.contains(a + 3);
            if (labelInside && (a % 4) == 0) {
                dwordsSplitForLabel++;     // only aligned positions could have been a `dd`
            }

            if ((a % 4) == 0 && a + 4 <= initEnd && !labelInside) {
                long value = readDword(a);
                String exact = nameFor(value);

                boolean referenced = hasDataReference(a);
                // A longer run is required to overrule the text heuristics than to stand alone.
                //
                // These two rules genuinely conflict, and each has been wrong in its own direction:
                // the text rule left half of vehLoadNameList - a 128-entry array of char* - as raw
                // bytes, and the table rule rewrote four real strings as pointers. Neither can
                // simply win.
                //
                // Length settles it. A real table is long: vehLoadNameList is 128 entries and the
                // CRT message arrays are ten or more. An accidental run through a string table is
                // four or five entries and then falls apart. So four entries is enough where
                // nothing suggests text, and eight are required where something does.
                // Text gets the strict rule; everything else may reach across one gap.
                boolean textish = insideString(a) || looksLikeStringData(a);
                int runLength = pointerTableRun(a, start, initEnd, !textish);
                boolean inTable = runLength >= (textish ? 8 : 4) || inNamedPointerObject(a);

                // A printf conversion vetoes everything, including the table rule.
                //
                // "%5d" survived the text check and was relocated anyway, because it sits among
                // enough plausible addresses to look like a pointer table. Nothing is worth that:
                // a format string decides how sprintf reads its arguments, so corrupting one can
                // turn a %d into a %s and hand it an integer to dereference. A real pointer whose
                // bytes happen to spell a valid conversion is possible; losing one is a stale
                // pointer, while losing a format string is a stack smash.
                if (looksLikeFormat(a, 4)) {
                    inTable = false;
                }

                if (value >= 0x400000L && inTable && !looksLikeFormat(a, 4)) {
                    // An ascending run of pointers. Structure beats coincidence.
                    if (exact != null) {
                        target = exact;
                    } else {
                        String[] near = nearest(value);
                        if (near != null) {
                            target = near[0];
                            addend = Long.parseLong(near[1]);
                            recoveredPointers++;
                        }
                    }
                } else if (value >= 0x400000L && !insideString(a) && !looksLikeStringData(a)
                        && !looksLikeFormat(a, 4)) {
                    if (exact != null && (referenced || inPointerRun(a, candidates))) {
                        target = exact;
                    } else if (referenced) {
                        String[] near = nearest(value);
                        if (near != null) {
                            target = near[0];
                            addend = Long.parseLong(near[1]);
                            recoveredPointers++;
                        }
                    } else if (exact != null) {
                        stringsProtected++;   // lone match, no reference: treated as data
                    }
                } else if (value >= 0x400000L) {
                    stringsProtected++;
                }
            }

            if (target != null) {
                flushRun(w, run, runLen);
                runLen = 0;
                w.println("    dd offset " + target + (addend != 0 ? " + " + addend : ""));
                dataPointers++;
                a += 4;
                continue;
            }

            int b = currentProgram.getMemory().getByte(toAddr(a)) & 0xFF;

            if (runLen > 0) {
                run.append(", ");
            }
            run.append(String.format("0%02Xh", b));
            runLen++;

            if (runLen == 16) {
                flushRun(w, run, runLen);
                runLen = 0;
            }

            a++;

            if (monitor.isCancelled()) {
                break;
            }
        }

        flushRun(w, run, runLen);

        println("  " + blockName + ": " + stringsProtected
                + " lone symbol-valued dwords left as data (no reference, no run)");

        if (initEnd < end) {
            w.println();
            w.println(".DATA?");
            w.println();

            for (long a = initEnd; a < end; ) {
                String sym = nameFor(a);
                if (sym != null) {
                    if (isLinkableName(sym)) {
                        w.println("PUBLIC " + sym);
                    }
                    w.println(sym + " LABEL BYTE");
                }

                long next = end;
                Long k = sortedSymbols.higherKey(a);
                if (k != null && k < end) {
                    next = k;
                }

                w.println("    db " + (next - a) + " dup (?)");
                a = next;
            }
        }
    }

    /** Did Ghidra's data analysis record a pointer originating at this address? */
    boolean hasDataReference(long a) {
        Reference[] refs = currentProgram.getReferenceManager().getReferencesFrom(toAddr(a));
        return refs != null && refs.length > 0;
    }

    void flushRun(PrintWriter w, StringBuilder run, int runLen) {
        if (runLen > 0) {
            w.println("    db " + run.toString());
        }
        run.setLength(0);
    }

    /**
     * Where `name` really ends: retail's section table gives its virtual size, while Ghidra's block
     * runs on through the file padding to the next page. Emitting the padding grows the section
     * past retail's, and the linker's own few bytes of .rdata then push .data a page high.
     * Anything addressed in the padding means the size is wrong, so that is refused.
     */
    long trimToSection(String name, long start, long blockEnd) throws Exception {
        long base = currentProgram.getImageBase().getOffset();
        long pe = base + readDword(base + 0x3C);
        int count = currentProgram.getMemory().getShort(toAddr(pe + 6)) & 0xFFFF;
        int optSize = currentProgram.getMemory().getShort(toAddr(pe + 20)) & 0xFFFF;
        long table = pe + 24 + optSize;

        for (int i = 0; i < count; i++) {
            long entry = table + 40L * i;
            byte[] raw = new byte[8];
            currentProgram.getMemory().getBytes(toAddr(entry), raw);
            if (!new String(raw, "US-ASCII").trim().equals(name)) {    // trim drops the NUL padding
                continue;
            }

            long end = Math.min(blockEnd, base + readDword(entry + 12) + readDword(entry + 8));
            Long sym = sortedSymbols.ceilingKey(end);
            boolean addressed = (sym != null && sym < blockEnd)
                    || neededDataLabels.stream().anyMatch(a -> a >= end && a < blockEnd);
            if (addressed) {
                throw new IllegalStateException(name + ": something is addressed past its virtual size");
            }
            return end;
        }
        return blockEnd;
    }

    long readDword(long a) throws Exception {
        return ((long) currentProgram.getMemory().getInt(toAddr(a))) & 0xFFFFFFFFL;
    }

    /**
     * Load the verified byte-to-mnemonic table, if one has been placed next to the output.
     *
     * Absence is not an error: the export simply falls back to the all-`db` form it produced
     * before this existed, which is correct, just unreadable. That matters because the table lives
     * in the repo at data/encodings.tsv while the script runs from C:/mm2ghidra/scripts, and the
     * headless launcher re-splits its arguments on spaces - so the repo path, which contains
     * "Dev Workspace", cannot be passed in. The table has to be copied to a space-free location
     * the same way this script is, and forgetting to do so must degrade rather than corrupt.
     */
    void loadVerifiedEncodings(File table) {
        if (!table.exists()) {
            println("no encodings.tsv next to the output - emitting all instructions as db.");
            println("  copy data/encodings.tsv there to get mnemonics; see tools/verify_encodings.py");
            return;
        }

        try (BufferedReader r = new BufferedReader(new FileReader(table))) {
            String line;
            while ((line = r.readLine()) != null) {
                if (line.isEmpty() || line.charAt(0) == '#') {
                    continue;
                }
                int tab = line.indexOf('\t');
                if (tab > 0) {
                    verifiedEncodings.put(line.substring(0, tab), line.substring(tab + 1));
                }
            }
        } catch (Exception e) {
            println("could not read " + table + ": " + e + " - emitting all instructions as db.");
            verifiedEncodings.clear();
            return;
        }

        println("verified encodings: " + verifiedEncodings.size());
    }

    static String hex(byte[] bytes) {
        StringBuilder sb = new StringBuilder(bytes.length * 2);
        for (byte b : bytes) {
            sb.append(Character.forDigit((b >> 4) & 0xF, 16));
            sb.append(Character.forDigit(b & 0xF, 16));
        }
        return sb.toString();
    }

    /**
     * True if any address strictly inside this instruction needs a label of its own.
     *
     * emitBytes can place a label between two `db` directives, so a reference INTO an instruction
     * - a jump table computed off a base, or a jump into the middle of an instruction, both of
     * which this binary really does - resolves fine in the byte form. A single mnemonic line has
     * nowhere to put that label, and MASM would report the reference as an undefined symbol.
     *
     * So an instruction with an interior label keeps its bytes. There are few of them and they are
     * exactly the places where the byte form is the honest rendering anyway.
     */
    boolean hasInteriorLabel(long addr, int length) {
        for (int i = 1; i < length; i++) {
            long here = addr + i;
            if (neededLabels.contains(here) || symbolAt.containsKey(here)) {
                return true;
            }
        }
        return false;
    }

    void emitBytes(PrintWriter w, byte[] bytes, int from, int len) {
        emitBytes(w, bytes, from, len, -1);
    }

    /**
     * Emit a byte run, breaking it wherever a label has to land.
     *
     * Some references point INTO an instruction rather than at its start - a jump table computed
     * off a base, mostly. Those addresses still need a label, and because the output is raw bytes
     * rather than mnemonics a label can simply be placed between two `db` directives. Anything
     * that assumes labels only occur at instruction boundaries leaves them undefined.
     */
    void emitBytes(PrintWriter w, byte[] bytes, int from, int len, long baseAddr) {
        StringBuilder sb = new StringBuilder();
        int run = 0;

        for (int i = 0; i < len; i++) {
            if (baseAddr >= 0) {
                long here = baseAddr + from + i;

                if (i > 0 && (neededLabels.contains(here) || symbolAt.containsKey(here))) {
                    if (run > 0) {
                        w.println("    db " + sb);
                        sb.setLength(0);
                        run = 0;
                    }

                    String s = nameFor(here);
                    if (s != null) {
                        w.println(s + ":");
                    }
                    if (neededLabels.contains(here)) {
                        w.println(labelFor(here) + ":");
                    }
                }
            }

            if (run > 0) {
                sb.append(", ");
            }
            sb.append(String.format("0%02Xh", bytes[from + i] & 0xFF));
            run++;

            if (run == 16) {
                w.println("    db " + sb);
                sb.setLength(0);
                run = 0;
            }
        }

        if (run > 0) {
            w.println("    db " + sb);
        }
    }

    int findDword(byte[] bytes, int value) {
        for (int i = 0; i + 4 <= bytes.length; i++) {
            int v = (bytes[i] & 0xFF) | ((bytes[i + 1] & 0xFF) << 8)
                    | ((bytes[i + 2] & 0xFF) << 16) | ((bytes[i + 3] & 0xFF) << 24);

            if (v == value) {
                return i;
            }
        }

        return -1;
    }
}

```

`tools/ghidra/FindFuncs.java`:

```java
// Recover the functions VC6's frame-pointer omission hid from the analyser - on proof, not pattern.
//
// Gaps.java established the shape: 117 KB of .text belongs to no function, and NOT ONE of those
// runs opens with `push ebp; mov ebp, esp`. VC6 at /O2 omits the frame pointer, so these functions
// start with `sub esp, N`, `mov eax, fs:[0]` or a bare `mov eax, [esp+4]`, and Ghidra's MSVC
// function-start patterns are built around the frame prologue. Nothing is damaged or hidden; the
// functions simply do not look like what was searched for.
//
// A first version of this script walked each uncovered RUN and skipped leading padding to guess
// where the function began. That was the wrong shape twice over: VC6 also pads with multi-byte nops
// (`8B FF`, `8D 49 00`) which a 0x90/0xCC skip walks straight past, and a run frequently holds
// SEVERAL consecutive undiscovered functions, so examining only its first address can never find
// the rest. It also cannot tell a function from a switch jump table, and disassembling a jump table
// as code writes damage into the database that no later rule change can undo.
//
// So the rule here does not involve padding, prologues or run boundaries at all:
//
//     every dword in the image that points into .text is a candidate; if no function covers that
//     address, it is a function start.
//
// That is proof rather than resemblance. A dword holding a .text address is a vtable slot, a
// function table entry or a switch target - and a switch target already lies inside its own
// function's body, so it is excluded by the same test. It is also why a direct-call search found
// nothing: these are reached through tables, never by a `call rel32` the analyser could follow.
//
// Run with `apply` to create them. Without it the script only reports, so the evidence can be read
// before the database is written to.
//
// @category OpenMCM2

import java.io.File;
import java.io.PrintWriter;
import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.Collections;
import java.util.List;

import ghidra.app.script.GhidraScript;
import ghidra.program.model.address.Address;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.Function;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.Instruction;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.Listing;
import ghidra.program.model.mem.MemoryBlock;

public class FindFuncs extends GhidraScript {

    StringBuilder out = new StringBuilder();

    void say(String s) {
        println(s);
        out.append(s).append('\n');
    }

    @Override
    public void run() throws Exception {
        String outDir = "C:/mcm2ghidra/out";
        boolean apply = false;
        for (String s : getScriptArgs()) {
            if (s.equalsIgnoreCase("apply")) {
                apply = true;
            } else if (s.length() > 0) {
                outDir = s;
            }
        }
        new File(outDir).mkdirs();
        say("mode: " + (apply ? "APPLY (creating functions)" : "report only"));

        Listing listing = currentProgram.getListing();
        MemoryBlock text = currentProgram.getMemory().getBlock(".text");
        long tlo = text.getStart().getOffset(), thi = text.getEnd().getOffset();

        // ---- every dword in the image that points into .text ----
        List<Long> candidates = new ArrayList<>();
        long scanned = 0;
        for (String blockName : new String[]{".rdata", ".data", ".text"}) {
            MemoryBlock b = currentProgram.getMemory().getBlock(blockName);
            if (b == null || !b.isInitialized()) {
                continue;
            }
            long lo = b.getStart().getOffset(), hi = b.getEnd().getOffset();
            for (long p = lo; p + 3 <= hi; p += 4) {
                long v;
                try {
                    v = getInt(toAddr(p)) & 0xFFFFFFFFL;
                } catch (Exception e) {
                    continue;
                }
                scanned++;
                if (v >= tlo && v <= thi) {
                    candidates.add(v);
                }
            }
        }
        Collections.sort(candidates);
        List<Long> uniq = new ArrayList<>();
        long prev = -1;
        for (long v : candidates) {
            if (v != prev) {
                uniq.add(v);
                prev = v;
            }
        }
        say(String.format("dwords scanned            : %d", scanned));
        say(String.format("pointing into .text       : %d  (%d distinct)", candidates.size(), uniq.size()));

        // ---- classify each distinct target ----
        int atEntry = 0, insideBody = 0, uncovered = 0;
        List<Long> toMake = new ArrayList<>();
        for (long v : uniq) {
            Address at = toAddr(v);
            Function f = listing.getFunctionContaining(at);
            if (f == null) {
                uncovered++;
                toMake.add(v);
            } else if (f.getEntryPoint().getOffset() == v) {
                atEntry++;
            } else {
                insideBody++;      // a switch target, or a label inside a known function
            }
        }
        say(String.format("   already a function entry : %d", atEntry));
        say(String.format("   inside a known body      : %d  (switch targets and internal labels)", insideBody));
        say(String.format("   COVERED BY NOTHING       : %d  <- undiscovered functions", uncovered));
        say("");

        // A sample, so the claim can be checked by eye rather than taken on trust.
        say("first 25 of them, with their opening bytes:");
        for (int i = 0; i < Math.min(25, toMake.size()); i++) {
            long v = toMake.get(i);
            StringBuilder hex = new StringBuilder();
            for (int k = 0; k < 14; k++) {
                hex.append(String.format("%02X ", getByte(toAddr(v + k)) & 0xFF));
            }
            say(String.format("   0x%08X  %s", v, hex.toString().trim()));
        }

        if (apply) {
            // Two tiers, kept apart so the risk of each is visible and separable.
            //
            // TIER A is free of risk: the address is ALREADY the start of a disassembled
            // instruction, so nothing new is decoded and all that happens is a function gets drawn
            // round bytes Ghidra had already decided were code. 85.6% of the uncovered bytes are in
            // this state, so most of the recovery lands here.
            //
            // TIER B has to disassemble undefined bytes, and that is where a database can be
            // damaged past recovery: not every dword holding a .text-shaped value is a pointer, and
            // one that is not would have real bytes decoded as instructions at the wrong offset.
            // `0x00404031` in the sample opens `11 8B 56 48`, an `adc` - almost certainly a
            // coincidence rather than a function. Two guards apply. Ghidra will not disassemble
            // over an existing instruction, so a mid-instruction address simply refuses; and the
            // byte BEFORE a genuine function start is always a terminator or padding, never live
            // code, so anything else is skipped.
            int madeA = 0, madeB = 0, already = 0, refused = 0, notBoundary = 0;
            for (long v : toMake) {
                Address at = toAddr(v);
                try {
                    if (getFunctionContaining(at) != null) {
                        already++;          // absorbed by a function created earlier in this pass
                        continue;
                    }
                    boolean wasCode = listing.getInstructionAt(at) != null;
                    if (!wasCode) {
                        int before = v > tlo ? (getByte(toAddr(v - 1)) & 0xFF) : 0x90;
                        //  90 nop   CC int3   C3 ret   C2 ret n   E9/EB jmp   00 zero fill
                        boolean boundary = before == 0x90 || before == 0xCC || before == 0xC3
                                || before == 0xC2 || before == 0xE9 || before == 0xEB
                                || before == 0x00;
                        if (!boundary) {
                            notBoundary++;
                            continue;
                        }
                        disassemble(at);
                        if (listing.getInstructionAt(at) == null) {
                            refused++;
                            continue;
                        }
                    }
                    if (createFunction(at, null) != null) {
                        if (wasCode) {
                            madeA++;
                        } else {
                            madeB++;
                        }
                    } else {
                        refused++;
                    }
                } catch (Exception e) {
                    refused++;
                }
            }
            say("");
            say(String.format("tier A - already code, function drawn round it : %d", madeA));
            say(String.format("tier B - undefined bytes disassembled          : %d", madeB));
            say(String.format("absorbed by a function made earlier            : %d", already));
            say(String.format("skipped, previous byte is live code            : %d", notBoundary));
            say(String.format("refused by the disassembler                    : %d", refused));
        }

        PrintWriter w = new PrintWriter(
                new File(outDir, apply ? "findfuncs_applied.txt" : "findfuncs.txt"), "UTF-8");
        w.print(out);
        w.println();
        w.println("all uncovered pointer targets:");
        for (long v : toMake) {
            w.printf("0x%08X%n", v);
        }
        w.close();
        say("");
        say("wrote report to " + outDir);
    }
}

```

`tools/ghidra/Gaps.java`:

```java
// What is actually inside the parts of .text that no function covers?
//
// The census says 8.41% of .text belongs to no function, in runs as long as 5,984 bytes. That
// number on its own does not say what the bytes ARE, and the three possibilities need completely
// different work:
//
//   * already disassembled, just not bundled into a function - a cheap fix, create the function
//   * defined data - jump tables and constant pools, which is normal and wants no action
//   * undefined bytes - code the analyser never found, which is the expensive case and the one that
//     matters, because an unanalysed instruction's address operands are never symbolised
//
// A 5,984-byte jump table would hold 1,496 entries, so the large runs are very unlikely to be
// tables. This measures it rather than assuming, and dumps the head of the biggest runs plus a
// count of MSVC-6 prologues (55 8B EC = push ebp / mov ebp, esp) found inside them, which is the
// direct evidence for "this is undiscovered code".
//
// @category OpenMCM2

import java.io.File;
import java.io.PrintWriter;
import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.List;

import ghidra.app.script.GhidraScript;
import ghidra.program.model.address.Address;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.CodeUnit;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.Data;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.Function;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.Instruction;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.Listing;
import ghidra.program.model.mem.MemoryBlock;

public class Gaps extends GhidraScript {

    StringBuilder out = new StringBuilder();

    void say(String s) {
        println(s);
        out.append(s).append('\n');
    }

    @Override
    public void run() throws Exception {
        String outDir = "C:/mcm2ghidra/out";
        String[] args = getScriptArgs();
        if (args.length > 0 && args[0].length() > 0) {
            outDir = args[0];
        }
        new File(outDir).mkdirs();

        Listing listing = currentProgram.getListing();
        MemoryBlock text = currentProgram.getMemory().getBlock(".text");
        Address start = text.getStart();
        Address end = text.getEnd();

        // ---- collect the runs no function covers ----
        List<long[]> runs = new ArrayList<>();
        Address a = start;
        long gapStart = -1;
        while (a != null && a.compareTo(end) <= 0) {
            Function f = listing.getFunctionContaining(a);
            if (f != null) {
                if (gapStart >= 0) {
                    runs.add(new long[]{gapStart, a.getOffset() - gapStart});
                    gapStart = -1;
                }
                long len = f.getBody().getMaxAddress().getOffset() - a.getOffset() + 1;
                a = a.add(len);
                continue;
            }
            if (gapStart < 0) {
                gapStart = a.getOffset();
            }
            if (a.equals(end)) {
                break;
            }
            a = a.add(1);
        }
        if (gapStart >= 0) {
            runs.add(new long[]{gapStart, end.getOffset() - gapStart + 1});
        }

        // ---- classify every byte inside them ----
        long asInstr = 0, asData = 0, asUndef = 0;
        long prologues = 0;
        for (long[] r : runs) {
            long off = r[0];
            long remaining = r[1];
            while (remaining > 0) {
                Address at = toAddr(off);
                CodeUnit cu = listing.getCodeUnitContaining(at);
                long step;
                if (cu instanceof Instruction) {
                    step = Math.min(remaining, cu.getLength());
                    asInstr += step;
                } else if (cu instanceof Data && ((Data) cu).isDefined()) {
                    step = Math.min(remaining, cu.getLength());
                    asData += step;
                } else {
                    step = 1;
                    asUndef++;
                }
                if (step <= 0) {
                    step = 1;
                }
                off += step;
                remaining -= step;
            }
            // MSVC 6 frame prologue, the strongest cheap signal that a run holds real code
            for (long p = r[0]; p + 2 < r[0] + r[1]; p++) {
                if ((getByte(toAddr(p)) & 0xFF) == 0x55
                        && (getByte(toAddr(p + 1)) & 0xFF) == 0x8B
                        && (getByte(toAddr(p + 2)) & 0xFF) == 0xEC) {
                    prologues++;
                }
            }
        }

        long total = 0;
        for (long[] r : runs) {
            total += r[1];
        }
        say(String.format("uncovered .text: %d bytes in %d runs", total, runs.size()));
        say(String.format("   already instructions : %8d  (%.1f%%)", asInstr, 100.0 * asInstr / total));
        say(String.format("   defined data         : %8d  (%.1f%%)", asData, 100.0 * asData / total));
        say(String.format("   UNDEFINED bytes      : %8d  (%.1f%%)", asUndef, 100.0 * asUndef / total));
        say(String.format("   'push ebp; mov ebp,esp' sequences inside them: %d", prologues));
        say("");

        runs.sort((x, y) -> Long.compare(y[1], x[1]));
        say("the 20 largest runs, with the first 24 bytes of each:");
        for (int i = 0; i < Math.min(20, runs.size()); i++) {
            long o = runs.get(i)[0], n = runs.get(i)[1];
            StringBuilder hex = new StringBuilder();
            for (int k = 0; k < Math.min(24, n); k++) {
                hex.append(String.format("%02X ", getByte(toAddr(o + k)) & 0xFF));
            }
            CodeUnit cu = listing.getCodeUnitContaining(toAddr(o));
            String kind = cu instanceof Instruction ? "instr"
                    : (cu instanceof Data && ((Data) cu).isDefined()) ? "data" : "undef";
            say(String.format("   0x%08X %6d  %-5s  %s", o, n, kind, hex.toString().trim()));
        }

        PrintWriter w = new PrintWriter(new File(outDir, "gaps.txt"), "UTF-8");
        w.print(out);
        // the full list, for whatever fixes it
        w.println();
        w.println("all runs (address, length):");
        List<long[]> byAddr = new ArrayList<>(runs);
        byAddr.sort((x, y) -> Long.compare(x[0], y[0]));
        for (long[] r : byAddr) {
            w.printf("0x%08X %d%n", r[0], r[1]);
        }
        w.close();
        say("");
        say("wrote " + new File(outDir, "gaps.txt"));
    }
}

```

`tools/ghidra/Probe.java`:

```java
// Print everything Ghidra knows about a handful of addresses. Used to settle "what is actually
// there" questions without inferring them from the exported assembly.
//
// @category OpenMCM2

import ghidra.app.script.GhidraScript;
import ghidra.program.model.address.Address;
import ghidra.program.model.address.AddressRange;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.Function;
import ghidra.program.model.symbol.Reference;
import ghidra.program.model.symbol.Symbol;

public class Probe extends GhidraScript {

    @Override
    public void run() throws Exception {
        for (String s : getScriptArgs()) {
            long v = Long.parseLong(s.replace("0x", ""), 16);
            Address a = toAddr(v);
            println("---- 0x" + Long.toHexString(v) + " ----");
            Function f = getFunctionContaining(a);
            if (f == null) {
                println("  no function contains it");
            } else {
                println("  function        : " + f.getName());
                println("  entry           : " + f.getEntryPoint());
                println("  body            : " + f.getBody().getNumAddressRanges() + " range(s)");
                for (AddressRange r : f.getBody()) {
                    println("      " + r.getMinAddress() + " .. " + r.getMaxAddress());
                }
            }
            Symbol[] syms = getSymbols(null, null) == null ? null : null;
            Symbol prim = getSymbolAt(a);
            println("  primary symbol  : " + (prim == null ? "(none)" : prim.getName()));
            int nref = 0, ncall = 0;
            for (Reference r : getReferencesTo(a)) {
                nref++;
                if (r.getReferenceType().isCall()) {
                    ncall++;
                }
            }
            println("  references to it: " + nref + " (" + ncall + " calls)");
        }
    }
}

```

`tools/ghidra/RttiMcm2.java`:

```java
// Stage 1.3 - Parse MSVC RTTI directly (Ghidra's analyzer recovered almost nothing here).
//
// MSVC 32-bit layout:
//   TypeDescriptor            +0 pVFTable  +4 spare  +8 name(".?AVBike@@")
//   CompleteObjectLocator     +0 signature +4 offset +8 cdOffset
//                             +12 pTypeDescriptor +16 pClassHierarchyDescriptor
//   ClassHierarchyDescriptor  +0 signature +4 attributes +8 numBaseClasses +12 pBaseClassArray
//   BaseClassDescriptor       +0 pTypeDescriptor +4 numContainedBases +8 PMD(mdisp,pdisp,vdisp)
//   vftable                   [vftable-4] = pCompleteObjectLocator
//
// Emits classes.json to mcm2_re\out\.
// @category OpenMCM2

import java.io.File;
import java.io.PrintWriter;
import java.util.*;

import ghidra.app.script.GhidraScript;
import ghidra.program.model.address.Address;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.Function;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.FunctionManager;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.Instruction;
import ghidra.program.model.listing.Listing;
import ghidra.program.model.scalar.Scalar;
import ghidra.program.model.symbol.Reference;
import ghidra.program.model.mem.Memory;
import ghidra.program.model.mem.MemoryBlock;
import ghidra.program.model.symbol.Namespace;
import ghidra.program.model.symbol.SourceType;

public class RttiMcm2 extends GhidraScript {

    // Output directory; the constant is only a default, the script argument wins.
    String OUT = "C:/mcm2ghidra/out";

    static String esc(String s) {
        if (s == null) return "null";
        StringBuilder b = new StringBuilder("\"");
        for (char c : s.toCharArray()) {
            if (c == '"' || c == '\\') b.append('\\').append(c);
            else if (c < 0x20 || c > 0x7e) b.append(String.format("\\u%04x", (int) c));
            else b.append(c);
        }
        return b.append('"').toString();
    }

    /** ".?AVFoo@Bar@@" -> "Bar::Foo" */
    static String demangle(String raw) {
        String s = raw;
        if (s.startsWith(".?AV") || s.startsWith(".?AU")) s = s.substring(4);
        while (s.endsWith("@")) s = s.substring(0, s.length() - 1);
        String[] parts = s.split("@");
        if (parts.length <= 1) return s;
        List<String> l = new ArrayList<>(Arrays.asList(parts));
        Collections.reverse(l);
        return String.join("::", l);
    }

    Memory mem;
    List<byte[]> blockBytes = new ArrayList<>();
    List<Address> blockStarts = new ArrayList<>();
    long textStart = Long.MAX_VALUE, textEnd = 0;

    long readU32(Address a) throws Exception {
        return mem.getInt(a) & 0xFFFFFFFFL;
    }

    /** All addresses in initialized memory holding a 4-byte LE value present in `targets`. */
    Map<Long, List<Long>> findPointersTo(Set<Long> targets) {
        Map<Long, List<Long>> hits = new HashMap<>();
        for (int b = 0; b < blockBytes.size(); b++) {
            byte[] buf = blockBytes.get(b);
            long base = blockStarts.get(b).getOffset();
            for (int i = 0; i + 4 <= buf.length; i++) {
                long v = (buf[i] & 0xffL) | ((buf[i + 1] & 0xffL) << 8)
                       | ((buf[i + 2] & 0xffL) << 16) | ((buf[i + 3] & 0xffL) << 24);
                if (targets.contains(v))
                    hits.computeIfAbsent(v, k -> new ArrayList<>()).add(base + i);
            }
        }
        return hits;
    }

    @Override
    public void run() throws Exception {
        String[] _a = getScriptArgs();
        if (_a.length > 0 && _a[0].length() > 0) OUT = _a[0];
        new File(OUT).mkdirs();
        mem = currentProgram.getMemory();
        FunctionManager fm = currentProgram.getFunctionManager();

        for (MemoryBlock blk : mem.getBlocks()) {
            if (!blk.isInitialized()) continue;
            byte[] buf = new byte[(int) blk.getSize()];
            blk.getBytes(blk.getStart(), buf);
            blockBytes.add(buf);
            blockStarts.add(blk.getStart());
        }
        // Needed during vftable discovery, so compute it up front.
        for (MemoryBlock blk : mem.getBlocks()) {
            if (!blk.isExecute()) continue;
            textStart = Math.min(textStart, blk.getStart().getOffset());
            textEnd = Math.max(textEnd, blk.getEnd().getOffset());
        }
        println("STATS text_range=" + Long.toHexString(textStart) + "-" + Long.toHexString(textEnd));

        // ---- 1. TypeDescriptors, via their ".?AV"/".?AU" name strings ----
        Map<Long, String> typeDesc = new HashMap<>();   // TypeDescriptor addr -> class name
        for (int b = 0; b < blockBytes.size(); b++) {
            byte[] buf = blockBytes.get(b);
            long base = blockStarts.get(b).getOffset();
            for (int i = 0; i + 4 < buf.length; i++) {
                if (buf[i] != '.' || buf[i + 1] != '?' || buf[i + 2] != 'A') continue;
                char k = (char) buf[i + 3];
                if (k != 'V' && k != 'U') continue;
                int j = i;
                while (j < buf.length && buf[j] != 0) j++;
                String raw = new String(buf, i, j - i, "ASCII");
                if (!raw.endsWith("@@")) continue;
                typeDesc.put(base + i - 8, demangle(raw));   // name sits at TypeDescriptor+8
            }
        }
        println("STATS type_descriptors=" + typeDesc.size());

        // ---- 2. CompleteObjectLocators point at a TypeDescriptor from offset +12 ----
        Map<Long, String> cols = new HashMap<>();       // COL addr -> class name
        Map<Long, Long> colToTd = new HashMap<>();
        for (Map.Entry<Long, List<Long>> e : findPointersTo(typeDesc.keySet()).entrySet()) {
            for (long loc : e.getValue()) {
                long col = loc - 12;
                try {
                    if (readU32(toAddr(col)) != 0) continue;   // signature must be 0 (32-bit)
                } catch (Exception ex) { continue; }
                cols.put(col, typeDesc.get(e.getKey()));
                colToTd.put(col, e.getKey());
            }
        }
        println("STATS complete_object_locators=" + cols.size());

        // ---- 3. vftables sit 4 bytes after a pointer to their COL ----
        Map<String, List<Long>> classVftables = new TreeMap<>();
        Map<Long, Long> vftToCol = new HashMap<>();
        for (Map.Entry<Long, List<Long>> e : findPointersTo(cols.keySet()).entrySet()) {
            for (long loc : e.getValue()) {
                long vft = loc + 4;
                long slot0;
                try { slot0 = readU32(toAddr(vft)); }
                catch (Exception ex) { continue; }

                // Accept on "slot 0 points into code", NOT on "a function already
                // exists there". Virtual methods reachable only through a vtable are
                // precisely the ones auto-analysis never turned into functions, so
                // requiring one here silently discarded whole classes (Bike among them).
                if (slot0 < textStart || slot0 > textEnd) continue;
                if (fm.getFunctionAt(toAddr(slot0)) == null) {
                    try {
                        disassemble(toAddr(slot0));
                        createFunction(toAddr(slot0), null);
                    } catch (Exception ex) { /* keep the vftable regardless */ }
                }
                classVftables.computeIfAbsent(cols.get(e.getKey()), k -> new ArrayList<>()).add(vft);
                vftToCol.put(vft, e.getKey());
            }
        }
        println("STATS classes_with_vftable=" + classVftables.size());

        // ---- 4. Base classes, via the ClassHierarchyDescriptor ----
        Map<String, List<String>> bases = new TreeMap<>();
        for (Map.Entry<Long, String> e : cols.entrySet()) {
            try {
                long chd = readU32(toAddr(e.getKey() + 16));
                long n = readU32(toAddr(chd + 8));
                long arr = readU32(toAddr(chd + 12));
                if (n == 0 || n > 64) continue;
                List<String> bl = new ArrayList<>();
                for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
                    long bcd = readU32(toAddr(arr + i * 4L));
                    long td = readU32(toAddr(bcd));
                    String nm = typeDesc.get(td);
                    if (nm != null && !nm.equals(e.getValue())) bl.add(nm);
                }
                if (!bl.isEmpty()) bases.put(e.getValue(), bl);
            } catch (Exception ex) { /* malformed chain, skip */ }
        }
        println("STATS classes_with_bases=" + bases.size());

        // ---- 5. Walk vftable slots and emit ----
        // Virtual methods are often reachable ONLY through the vtable, so Ghidra's
        // auto-analysis never created functions for them. Create them here, and stop
        // the walk at the executable-section bounds or at the next vtable rather than
        // at the first address that happens to lack a function.
        Set<Long> vftStarts = new HashSet<>();
        for (List<Long> l : classVftables.values()) vftStarts.addAll(l);

        // Pass 1: walk every vtable, creating missing functions, WITHOUT naming anything yet.
        Map<String, List<long[]>> walked = new TreeMap<>();   // class -> list of {vftAddr, targets...}
        int totalSlots = 0, created = 0;
        for (Map.Entry<String, List<Long>> e : classVftables.entrySet()) {
            List<long[]> vts = new ArrayList<>();
            for (long vft : e.getValue()) {
                List<Long> targets = new ArrayList<>();
                for (int i = 0; i < 512; i++) {
                    long slotAddr = vft + i * 4L;
                    if (i > 0 && vftStarts.contains(slotAddr)) break;   // next vtable begins
                    long target;
                    try { target = readU32(toAddr(slotAddr)); } catch (Exception ex) { break; }
                    if (target < textStart || target > textEnd) break;  // out of code

                    Address ta = toAddr(target);
                    Function fn = fm.getFunctionAt(ta);
                    if (fn == null) {
                        try {
                            disassemble(ta);
                            fn = createFunction(ta, null);
                            if (fn != null) created++;
                        } catch (Exception ex) { /* fall through */ }
                    }
                    if (fn == null) break;
                    targets.add(target);
                    totalSlots++;
                }
                long[] row = new long[targets.size() + 1];
                row[0] = vft;
                for (int i = 0; i < targets.size(); i++) row[i + 1] = targets.get(i);
                vts.add(row);
            }
            walked.put(e.getKey(), vts);
        }
        println("STATS total_vslots=" + totalSlots);
        println("STATS functions_created=" + created);

        // Pass 2: decide the owner of each method. A method appearing at the same slot in
        // several vtables was inherited, not overridden, so it belongs to the most basal
        // class that has it (fewest base classes). Alphabetical tie-break for determinism.
        Map<Long, String> owner = new HashMap<>();
        Map<Long, Integer> ownerSlot = new HashMap<>();
        Map<Long, Integer> ownerDepth = new HashMap<>();
        for (Map.Entry<String, List<long[]>> e : walked.entrySet()) {
            String cls = e.getKey();
            List<String> bl = bases.get(cls);
            int depth = (bl == null) ? 0 : bl.size();
            for (long[] row : e.getValue()) {
                for (int i = 1; i < row.length; i++) {
                    long t = row[i];
                    Integer cur = ownerDepth.get(t);
                    if (cur == null || depth < cur || (depth == cur && cls.compareTo(owner.get(t)) < 0)) {
                        owner.put(t, cls);
                        ownerDepth.put(t, depth);
                        ownerSlot.put(t, i - 1);
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        int inherited = totalSlots - owner.size();
        println("STATS distinct_methods=" + owner.size());
        println("STATS inherited_slot_entries=" + inherited);

        // Pass 3: apply names (idempotent - only touches FUN_* and previously generated vf_*).
        int renamed = 0;
        for (Map.Entry<Long, String> e : owner.entrySet()) {
            Function fn = fm.getFunctionAt(toAddr(e.getKey()));
            if (fn == null) continue;
            String local = fn.getName();
            if (!(local.startsWith("FUN_") || local.matches("vf_\\d+"))) continue;
            try {
                Namespace ns = currentProgram.getSymbolTable().getOrCreateNameSpace(
                        currentProgram.getGlobalNamespace(), e.getValue(), SourceType.IMPORTED);
                fn.setParentNamespace(ns);
                fn.setName("vf_" + ownerSlot.get(e.getKey()), SourceType.IMPORTED);
                renamed++;
            } catch (Exception ex) { /* keep existing name */ }
        }
        println("STATS methods_named=" + renamed);

        // Pass 3b: recover class sizes from the `operator new` argument at construction
        // sites. Pattern:  push <size> / call operator new / ... / mov ecx,eax / call ctor
        // A constructor is identified by it writing a known vftable pointer into `this`.
        Map<Long, String> vftToClass = new HashMap<>();
        for (Map.Entry<String, List<Long>> e : classVftables.entrySet())
            for (long v : e.getValue()) vftToClass.put(v, e.getKey());

        Map<Long, String> ctorToClass = new HashMap<>();   // ctor entry -> class
        for (Map.Entry<Long, String> e : vftToClass.entrySet()) {
            for (Reference r : currentProgram.getReferenceManager().getReferencesTo(toAddr(e.getKey()))) {
                Function fn = fm.getFunctionContaining(r.getFromAddress());
                if (fn == null) continue;
                ctorToClass.putIfAbsent(fn.getEntryPoint().getOffset(), e.getValue());
            }
        }
        println("STATS candidate_constructors=" + ctorToClass.size());

        // The game uses its own allocator (BlockAllocator.cpp), so keying off `operator new`
        // finds almost nothing. Work backwards from CONSTRUCTOR call sites instead, which is
        // allocator-agnostic. Heap construction in MSVC looks like:
        //     push <size> / call <allocator> / add esp,4 / test eax,eax / je .. / mov ecx,eax / call <ctor>
        // Requiring the `mov ecx,eax` keeps stack-allocated objects (no allocation) out.
        Listing listing = currentProgram.getListing();
        Map<String, Map<Long, Integer>> sizeVotes = new TreeMap<>();
        Map<Long, Integer> allocatorVotes = new HashMap<>();
        int ctorSites = 0, matchedSites = 0;

        for (Map.Entry<Long, String> ce : ctorToClass.entrySet()) {
            for (Reference r : currentProgram.getReferenceManager()
                    .getReferencesTo(toAddr(ce.getKey()))) {
                if (!r.getReferenceType().isCall()) continue;
                Instruction call = listing.getInstructionAt(r.getFromAddress());
                if (call == null) continue;
                ctorSites++;

                boolean sawThisFromEax = false;
                Long size = null, allocator = null;
                Instruction p = call;
                for (int k = 0; k < 16 && p != null; k++) {
                    p = p.getPrevious();
                    if (p == null) break;
                    String flat = p.toString().replace(" ", "").toUpperCase();

                    if (flat.equals("MOVECX,EAX")) { sawThisFromEax = true; continue; }

                    if (p.getMnemonicString().equalsIgnoreCase("CALL") && sawThisFromEax) {
                        Reference[] fr = p.getReferencesFrom();
                        for (Reference a : fr)
                            if (a.getReferenceType().isCall()) allocator = a.getToAddress().getOffset();
                        // the size is pushed immediately before the allocator call
                        Instruction q = p;
                        for (int j = 0; j < 4 && q != null; j++) {
                            q = q.getPrevious();
                            if (q == null) break;
                            if (!q.getMnemonicString().equalsIgnoreCase("PUSH")) continue;
                            Object[] ops = q.getOpObjects(0);
                            if (ops.length == 1 && ops[0] instanceof Scalar)
                                size = ((Scalar) ops[0]).getUnsignedValue();
                            break;
                        }
                        break;
                    }
                }
                if (size == null || size == 0 || size > 0x10000) continue;
                matchedSites++;
                sizeVotes.computeIfAbsent(ce.getValue(), x -> new HashMap<>()).merge(size, 1, Integer::sum);
                if (allocator != null) allocatorVotes.merge(allocator, 1, Integer::sum);
            }
        }
        println("STATS ctor_call_sites=" + ctorSites);
        println("STATS ctor_sites_with_alloc=" + matchedSites);
        for (Map.Entry<Long, Integer> a : allocatorVotes.entrySet()) {
            Function af = fm.getFunctionAt(toAddr(a.getKey()));
            println("ALLOCATOR " + toAddr(a.getKey()) + " "
                    + (af == null ? "?" : af.getName(true)) + " sites=" + a.getValue());
        }

        Map<String, Long> classSize = new TreeMap<>();
        Map<String, Integer> classSizeConf = new TreeMap<>();
        int ambiguous = 0;
        for (Map.Entry<String, Map<Long, Integer>> e : sizeVotes.entrySet()) {
            long best = -1; int bestN = 0, total = 0;
            for (Map.Entry<Long, Integer> v : e.getValue().entrySet()) {
                total += v.getValue();
                if (v.getValue() > bestN) { bestN = v.getValue(); best = v.getKey(); }
            }
            if (e.getValue().size() > 1) ambiguous++;
            classSize.put(e.getKey(), best);
            classSizeConf.put(e.getKey(), bestN == total ? bestN : -bestN);  // negative = disputed
        }
        println("STATS classes_with_size=" + classSize.size());
        println("STATS classes_size_disputed=" + ambiguous);

        // Pass 4: emit
        StringBuilder sb = new StringBuilder("{\n");
        boolean first = true;
        for (Map.Entry<String, List<long[]>> e : walked.entrySet()) {
            String cls = e.getKey();
            if (!first) sb.append(",\n");
            first = false;
            sb.append(" ").append(esc(cls)).append(": {");
            sb.append("\"size\":").append(classSize.containsKey(cls) ? classSize.get(cls) : "null")
              .append(",\"size_votes\":").append(classSizeConf.containsKey(cls) ? classSizeConf.get(cls) : 0)
              .append(",");
            List<String> bl = bases.get(cls);
            sb.append("\"bases\":[");
            if (bl != null) {
                List<String> q = new ArrayList<>();
                for (String x : bl) q.add(esc(x));
                sb.append(String.join(",", q));
            }
            sb.append("],\"vftables\":[");
            List<String> vts = new ArrayList<>();
            for (long[] row : e.getValue()) {
                List<String> slots = new ArrayList<>();
                for (int i = 1; i < row.length; i++) {
                    Function fn = fm.getFunctionAt(toAddr(row[i]));
                    String nm = (fn == null) ? "?" : fn.getName(true);
                    slots.add("{\"slot\":" + (i - 1) + ",\"addr\":\"" + toAddr(row[i])
                            + "\",\"name\":" + esc(nm)
                            + ",\"owner\":" + esc(owner.get(row[i]))
                            + ",\"introduced\":" + (cls.equals(owner.get(row[i]))) + "}");
                }
                vts.add("{\"addr\":\"" + toAddr(row[0]) + "\",\"slot_count\":" + slots.size()
                        + ",\"slots\":[" + String.join(",", slots) + "]}");
            }
            sb.append(String.join(",", vts)).append("]}");
        }
        sb.append("\n}\n");

        try (PrintWriter w = new PrintWriter(OUT + "\\classes.json", "UTF-8")) { w.print(sb); }
        println("WROTE " + OUT + "\\classes.json");

        List<Map.Entry<String, List<Long>>> top = new ArrayList<>(classVftables.entrySet());
        top.sort((a, b) -> b.getValue().size() - a.getValue().size());
        for (int i = 0; i < Math.min(15, top.size()); i++)
            println("CLASS " + top.get(i).getKey() + " vftables=" + top.get(i).getValue().size());
    }
}

```

`tools/ghidra/cycle.sh`:

```sh
#!/bin/sh
# Export game.asm from Ghidra, hand the ported functions to C, assemble, compile code/mcm2, link,
# and check the result against retail - as ONE command.
#
# These run together on purpose. Run separately, a stale executable has twice been measured as if
# it were fresh, and that failure is silent: every check passes, because the stale build is
# internally consistent. It is simply not the build the change was meant to produce.
#
# The scripts in tools/ghidra are the master copies. Ghidra runs them from C:/mcm2ghidra/scripts,
# because analyzeHeadless.bat splits its arguments on spaces and this tree's path has one.
set -e
HERE=$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)
REPO=$(cd "$HERE/../.." && pwd)
WORK=/c/mcm2ghidra
GH="${GHIDRA_HEADLESS:-$GHIDRA_INSTALL_DIR/support/analyzeHeadless.bat}"
[ -f "$GH" ] || { echo "set GHIDRA_INSTALL_DIR to a Ghidra 12.1 install, or GHIDRA_HEADLESS to its analyzeHeadless.bat"; exit 1; }
ML=$(ls -d "/c/Program Files/Microsoft Visual Studio"/*/*/VC/Tools/MSVC/*/bin/Hostx64/x86/ml.exe | tail -1)

mkdir -p "$WORK/scripts" "$WORK/out"
cp "$HERE"/*.java "$WORK/scripts/"

echo "=== 1/6 export ==="
"$GH" C:/mcm2ghidra/proj MCM2 -process Mcm2.exe -noanalysis \
    -scriptPath C:/mcm2ghidra/scripts -postScript ExportAsm.java C:/mcm2ghidra/out 2>&1 \
    | grep -iE "ExportAsm.java>|ERROR|Exception|error:" | sed 's/^INFO *//' \
    | grep -viE "^ExportAsm.java>   (code|data): " || true

echo "=== 2/6 ports ==="
cd "$REPO"
py -3.12 tools/port.py

echo "=== 3/6 assemble ==="
cd "$WORK/out"
"$ML" -c -coff -Fogame.obj game.asm 2>&1 | grep -viE "^(Microsoft|Copyright|$| Assembling)" || true
ls -l "$WORK/out/game.obj"

echo "=== 4/6 compile ==="
cd "$REPO"
py -3.12 tools/compile.py
[ -f build/game.res ] || py -3.12 tools/resources.py

echo "=== 5/6 link ==="
py -3.12 tools/link.py

echo "=== 6/6 verify against retail ==="
py -3.12 tools/verify.py
echo CYCLE_DONE

```

`tools/ghidra/reimport.sh`:

```sh
#!/bin/sh
# Rebuild the MCM2 Ghidra database from scratch, to the state everything else assumes.
#
# WHY THIS SCRIPT HAS TO EXIST, AND HAS TO STAY CORRECT
#
# Ghidra's database is state, and every pass that clears or creates a listing writes back into it.
# On the MM2 port an experimental rule cleared roughly 32,000 listings; the clears persisted after
# the rule was reverted, and much later the exporter read the middle of a live instruction as a
# pointer and relocated it. No rule change could undo that - the evidence it needed had been deleted
# from the database. So: measure any rule change against a freshly imported database, and keep this
# script able to produce one.
#
# WHY THE RECOVERY PASS IS PART OF THE IMPORT, NOT AN EXTRA
#
# Ghidra's own analysis leaves 8.41% of .text belonging to no function, in runs up to 5,984 bytes.
# That is not a defect in Ghidra: Mcm2.exe was built by VC6 at /O2, which OMITS THE FRAME POINTER,
# so its functions open with `sub esp, N`, `mov eax, fs:[0]` or a bare `mov eax, [esp+4]`, and the
# MSVC function-start patterns are built around `push ebp; mov ebp, esp`. There is not one of those
# in the whole uncovered set. The functions are reached only through vtables, so no direct call
# leads to them either.
#
# FindFuncs.java recovers them on proof - every dword in the image that points into .text and that
# no function covers - and takes coverage from 91.59% to 98.54%, 4,170 functions to 5,658. A
# database without that pass is a different database, so the pass belongs here.
#
# The scripts in tools/ghidra are the master copies; they are copied to C:/mcm2ghidra/scripts first,
# because analyzeHeadless.bat splits its arguments on spaces and this tree's path has one.
set -e
HERE=$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)
GH="${GHIDRA_HEADLESS:-$GHIDRA_INSTALL_DIR/support/analyzeHeadless.bat}"
[ -f "$GH" ] || { echo "set GHIDRA_INSTALL_DIR to a Ghidra 12.1 install, or GHIDRA_HEADLESS to its analyzeHeadless.bat"; exit 1; }
PROJ=C:/mcm2ghidra/proj
SCRIPTS=C:/mcm2ghidra/scripts
OUT=C:/mcm2ghidra/out

mkdir -p /c/mcm2ghidra/scripts /c/mcm2ghidra/out
cp "$HERE"/*.java /c/mcm2ghidra/scripts/

if [ -d /c/mcm2ghidra/proj.old ]; then rm -rf /c/mcm2ghidra/proj.old; fi
if [ -d /c/mcm2ghidra/proj ]; then mv /c/mcm2ghidra/proj /c/mcm2ghidra/proj.old; fi
mkdir -p /c/mcm2ghidra/proj

echo "=== 1/4 importing and analysing Mcm2.exe ==="
"$GH" "$PROJ" MCM2 -import C:/mcm2ghidra/bin/Mcm2.exe 2>&1 \
    | grep -iE "ERROR|Exception|Analysis succeeded|Import succeeded" | tail -10

echo "=== 2/4 census before recovery ==="
"$GH" "$PROJ" MCM2 -process Mcm2.exe -noanalysis -scriptPath "$SCRIPTS" \
    -postScript Census.java "$OUT" >/dev/null 2>&1
cp "$OUT/census.txt" "$OUT/census_before.txt"
grep -E "^functions|covered by funcs|NOT covered" "$OUT/census_before.txt"

echo "=== 3/4 recovering the frame-pointer-omitted functions ==="
"$GH" "$PROJ" MCM2 -process Mcm2.exe -noanalysis -scriptPath "$SCRIPTS" \
    -postScript FindFuncs.java "$OUT" apply >/dev/null 2>&1
grep -E "^tier |^absorbed|^skipped|^refused" "$OUT/findfuncs_applied.txt"

echo "=== 4/4 census after recovery ==="
"$GH" "$PROJ" MCM2 -process Mcm2.exe -noanalysis -scriptPath "$SCRIPTS" \
    -postScript Census.java "$OUT" >/dev/null 2>&1
grep -E "^functions|covered by funcs|NOT covered" "$OUT/census.txt"

echo "=== import done ==="
echo "RTTI is NOT run here - it takes ~10 minutes and changes no listings."
echo "Run it separately when class names are wanted:"
echo "  \"\$GH\" $PROJ MCM2 -process Mcm2.exe -noanalysis -scriptPath $SCRIPTS \\"
echo "      -postScript RttiMcm2.java $OUT"

```

`tools/imports.py`:

```py
"""List a PE32's imports exactly as its own import table states them."""
import struct, sys

path = sys.argv[1]
d = open(path, "rb").read()
pe = struct.unpack_from("<I", d, 0x3C)[0]
nsec = struct.unpack_from("<H", d, pe + 6)[0]
optsz = struct.unpack_from("<H", d, pe + 20)[0]
imgbase = struct.unpack_from("<I", d, pe + 24 + 28)[0]
imp_rva, imp_sz = struct.unpack_from("<II", d, pe + 24 + 96 + 8)
secs = []
for i in range(nsec):
    o = pe + 24 + optsz + i * 40
    vsz, va, rsz, rptr = struct.unpack_from("<IIII", d, o + 8)
    secs.append((va, va + max(vsz, rsz), rptr))


def off(rva):
    for va, ve, rp in secs:
        if va <= rva < ve:
            return rp + (rva - va)
    return None


def cstr(o):
    e = d.index(b"\0", o)
    return d[o:e].decode("latin-1")


total = 0
i = 0
rows = []
while True:
    o = off(imp_rva) + i * 20
    oft, tstamp, fwd, name_rva, first = struct.unpack_from("<IIIII", d, o)
    if not name_rva:
        break
    dll = cstr(off(name_rva))
    thunk = off(oft or first)
    n, ords = 0, 0
    j = 0
    while True:
        v = struct.unpack_from("<I", d, thunk + j * 4)[0]
        if not v:
            break
        if v & 0x80000000:
            ords += 1
        n += 1
        j += 1
    rows.append((dll, n, ords, imgbase + first))
    total += n
    i += 1
rows.sort(key=lambda r: -r[1])
print("%-20s %6s %6s  %s" % ("DLL", "funcs", "byord", "IAT VA"))
for dll, n, o_, iat in rows:
    print("%-20s %6d %6d  0x%08X" % (dll, n, o_, iat))
print("-- %d DLLs, %d imported functions" % (len(rows), total))

```

`tools/link.py`:

```py
# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
"""Link game.obj into a runnable executable.

At 0% reimplemented this produces a binary that IS the original game: every function is still the
original machine code, merely assembled and linked by us rather than shipped by Rainbow Studios.
That is the point. It is the baseline the port is measured against, and it is what makes each
ported function individually testable the day it is written.

One setting is not optional:
```
