```py
  -FIXED -BASE:0x400000   Mcm2.exe has IMAGE_FILE_RELOCS_STRIPPED set and no base-relocation
                          table, so it can only ever load at 0x400000. Anything else and every
                          absolute address baked into the code is wrong - silently, because there
                          is no relocation data for the loader to complain about.

No import libraries are linked. Retail's import table is part of the .rdata the assembly emits
and every import is called through its slot there, so the linker has nothing to import and builds
no table of its own. That keeps .text, .rdata and .data at their retail sizes and addresses, so
every pointer in the data is right as it stands. The PE header is then pointed at retail's import
tables (adopt_retail_header), and the build is refused if any of those sections has moved.

Sections of our own (compiled code, see build/obj) land after .data, and .rsrc after them: it is
built by the linker from build/game.res (tools/resources.py), which keeps its RVAs right wherever
it ends up.

Compiled code reaches the game through two conventions, both bound here with /alternatename:

  Win32 calls         `__imp__CreateFileA@28`, as the compiler spells a __stdcall import, is
                      bound to the game's own `__imp__CreateFileA` slot. A function the game does
                      not import cannot be called this way (it would need an import table) and
                      stops the link; resolve it at runtime instead.
  game_<name>         a C declaration of `game_entry` binds to the game's label `entry`.
  ports               a function tools/port.py handed to C: the game's name for it binds to the C
                      definition, whatever its calling convention made of the name.

If the compiled code defines `open_start`, that is the entry point; it runs the game's own.

THE CRT IS NOT LINKED IN. Mcm2.exe statically linked its own 1999 CRT and every one of those
routines is already in game.obj under its ordinary C name - `_memcpy`, `_malloc`, `_sprintf`. The
MSVC and UCRT library directories are on the search list only so a symbol game.obj does NOT define
can be satisfied rather than becoming a hard link error; one that needs a Windows import fails to
link, since there are no import libraries. Nothing that needs CRT initialisation will work
regardless: the entry point is the game's own 1999 startup and the modern initialiser never runs.
"""
import glob
import os
import re
import struct
import subprocess
import sys

import coff
from paths import RETAIL

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
ROOT = os.path.dirname(HERE)

OBJ = os.environ.get("MCM2_OBJ", r"C:\mcm2ghidra\out\game.obj")
ASM = os.environ.get("MCM2_ASM", r"C:\mcm2ghidra\out\game.asm")
OUT = os.path.join(ROOT, "build", "OpenMCM2.exe")

IMAGE_BASE = "0x400000"

# Which PUBLIC symbol is the entry point depends on the Ghidra database: it creates its own `entry`
# label at the image entry point, and a symbol recovered from elsewhere can win as primary. So the
# name is read out of the assembly rather than assumed. Getting this wrong does not say so - the
# linker falls back to _WinMainCRTStartup and reports THAT as the unresolved symbol, which points
# nowhere near the actual cause.
ENTRY_CANDIDATES = ("entry", "start", "WinMainCRTStartup")

RES = os.path.join(ROOT, "build", "game.res")
PORTS = os.path.join(ROOT, "build", "ports.tsv")

# The sections the game addresses, which must stay exactly where retail has them.
FIXED_SECTIONS = (".text", ".rdata", ".data")


# The data directories taken from retail: their tables are emitted verbatim at retail addresses.
DIR_IMPORT, DIR_IAT = 1, 12

# The OS and subsystem versions (four words at optional header +40 and +48). Windows only
# virtualises the registry for an executable that declares a pre-Vista version, and the game keeps
# its settings, its EULA acceptance and its profiles under HKLM: declaring the linker's 6.0 makes
# every one of those writes fail, so it asks again on every launch.
VERSIONS = ((40, 4), (48, 4))


def find(pattern, what):
    hits = sorted(glob.glob(pattern))
    if not hits:
        sys.exit("could not find %s (looked for %s)" % (what, pattern))
    return hits[-1]


def cpp_objects():
    """Compiled reimplementations. These take precedence over the stripped assembly."""
    return sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(ROOT, "build", "obj", "*.obj")))


def game_symbols():
    """(the game's import names, its public labels), as defined in the assembly."""
    imports, publics = set(), set()
    with open(ASM, encoding="utf-8", errors="replace") as f:
        for line in f:
            if line.startswith("PUBLIC "):
                name = line[7:].strip()
                (imports if name.startswith("__imp__") else publics).add(name)
    return imports, publics


def bindings(objects):
    """/alternatename options binding the compiled code's undefined symbols to the game."""
    imports, publics = game_symbols()
    options, missing = set(), set()
    for path in objects:
        with open(path, "rb") as f:
            data = f.read()
        for name, section, storage in coff.symbols(data):
            if section != 0 or storage != 2:     # defined, or not external
                continue
            m = re.match(r"^__imp__(\w+)@\d+$", name)
            if m:
                target = "__imp__" + m.group(1)
                (options.add("/ALTERNATENAME:%s=%s" % (name, target)) if target in imports
                 else missing.add("%s (not imported by the game)" % m.group(1)))
                continue
            m = re.match(r"^[_@]game_(\w+?)(@\d+)?$", name)
            if m:
                target = m.group(1)
                (options.add("/ALTERNATENAME:%s=%s" % (name, target)) if target in publics
                 else missing.add("%s (no such game label)" % target))
    if missing:
        sys.exit("compiled code needs symbols the game does not have:\n  " + "\n  ".join(sorted(missing)))
    return sorted(options) + port_bindings(objects)


def port_bindings(objects):
    """/alternatename options binding each ported function's game name to its C definition."""
    if not os.path.exists(PORTS):
        return []

    defined = {}
    for path in objects:
        with open(path, "rb") as f:
            for name, section, storage in coff.symbols(f.read()):
                if section > 0 and storage == 2:
                    m = re.match(r"^[_@](\w+?)(@\d+)?$", name)
                    if m:
                        defined[m.group(1)] = name

    options = []
    with open(PORTS, encoding="utf-8") as f:
        for line in f:
            address, game_name, c_name = line.split()
            if c_name not in defined:
                sys.exit("0x%s is ported to %s, which no object defines" % (address, c_name))
            options.append("/ALTERNATENAME:%s=%s" % (game_name, defined[c_name]))
    return options


def defines(objects, name):
    for path in objects:
        with open(path, "rb") as f:
            if any(n == name and section > 0 for n, section, _ in coff.symbols(f.read())):
                return True
    return False


def pe_layout(data):
    """(offset of the data directory table, {section name: (virtual address, virtual size)})."""
    pe = struct.unpack_from("<I", data, 0x3C)[0]
    count, = struct.unpack_from("<H", data, pe + 6)
    opt_size, = struct.unpack_from("<H", data, pe + 20)
    table = pe + 24 + opt_size
    sections = {}
    for i in range(count):
        name, size, va = struct.unpack_from("<8sII", data, table + 40 * i)
        sections[name.rstrip(b"\0").decode()] = (va, size)
    return pe + 24 + 96, sections


def adopt_retail_header(path):
    """Point the built image at retail's import and IAT tables, and declare retail's versions.

    Those tables, and every pointer in the game's data, are only valid if .text, .rdata and .data
    are at their retail addresses - which is checked first, because a section that moved would
    leave them pointing at whatever now sits there.
    """
    with open(RETAIL, "rb") as f:
        retail = f.read()
    with open(path, "rb") as f:
        built = bytearray(f.read())

    retail_dirs, retail_sections = pe_layout(retail)
    built_dirs, built_sections = pe_layout(built)
    retail_opt, built_opt = retail_dirs - 96, built_dirs - 96

    moved = ["%s 0x%X -> 0x%X" % (name, retail_sections[name][0], built_sections.get(name, (0, 0))[0])
             for name in FIXED_SECTIONS if built_sections.get(name, (0, 0))[0] != retail_sections[name][0]]
    if moved:
        sys.exit("sections are not at their retail addresses: " + ", ".join(moved))

    # Compiled code belongs in .open. Anything that reached .data - a common symbol, say - moves
    # nothing, so the check above cannot see it.
    if built_sections[".data"][1] != retail_sections[".data"][1]:
        sys.exit(".data is 0x%X bytes against retail's 0x%X: compiled code put something in it - see the map"
                 % (built_sections[".data"][1], retail_sections[".data"][1]))

    for index in (DIR_IMPORT, DIR_IAT):
        entry = retail_dirs + 8 * index
        built[built_dirs + 8 * index:built_dirs + 8 * index + 8] = retail[entry:entry + 8]

    for offset, size in VERSIONS:
        built[built_opt + offset:built_opt + offset + size] = retail[retail_opt + offset:retail_opt + offset + size]

    with open(path, "wb") as f:
        f.write(built)


def entry_point():
    if not os.path.exists(ASM):
        sys.exit("no assembly at %s" % ASM)
    declared = set()
    with open(ASM, encoding="utf-8", errors="replace") as f:
        for line in f:
            if line.startswith("PUBLIC "):
                declared.add(line[7:].strip())
    for name in ENTRY_CANDIDATES:
        if name in declared:
            return name
    sys.exit("no entry point in %s - looked for %s" % (ASM, ", ".join(ENTRY_CANDIDATES)))


def main():
    if not os.path.exists(OBJ):
        sys.exit("no object at %s - assemble game.asm first" % OBJ)
    if not os.path.exists(RES):
        sys.exit("no resources at %s - run tools/resources.py first" % RES)

    link = os.environ.get("LINK_EXE") or find(
        r"C:\Program Files\Microsoft Visual Studio\*\*\VC\Tools\MSVC"
        r"\*\bin\Hostx64\x86\link.exe", "link.exe")

    # link.exe sits at <msvc>/bin/Hostx64/x86/link.exe, so the toolchain root is four levels up.
    msvc_root = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(link))))
    msvc_lib = os.path.join(msvc_root, "lib", "x86")
    ucrt_lib = find(r"C:\Program Files (x86)\Windows Kits\10\Lib\*\ucrt\x86", "the UCRT x86 libraries")

    objects = cpp_objects()
    entry = "open_start" if defines(objects, "_open_start") else entry_point()
    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(OUT), exist_ok=True)

    cmd = [
        link,
        "-NOLOGO",
        "-MACHINE:X86",
        "-SUBSYSTEM:WINDOWS",
        "-BASE:" + IMAGE_BASE,
        "-FIXED",
        "-ENTRY:" + entry,

        # uuid.lib is requested by a CRT directive and holds nothing but COM interface GUID
        # constants, which this image already carries in its own data - they are emitted out of
        # Mcm2.exe's .rdata. It lives only in the SDK's um directory, the one path that must stay
        # off the search list, so it is excluded by name rather than opening that door.
        "-NODEFAULTLIB:uuid.lib",

        # A map, so an address in appended C++ can be named. Without one a crash inside a
        # reimplementation is just a number.
        "-MAP:" + os.path.join(ROOT, "build", "OpenMCM2.map"),

        "-OUT:" + OUT,
        "-LIBPATH:" + msvc_lib,
        "-LIBPATH:" + ucrt_lib,
        OBJ,
        RES,
    ] + objects + bindings(objects)

    print("link.exe : %s" % link)
    print("entry    : %s" % entry)
    print("object   : %s (%d bytes)" % (OBJ, os.path.getsize(OBJ)))
    res = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True)
    out = (res.stdout or "") + (res.stderr or "")
    if res.returncode != 0:
        # Unresolved externals come in thousands when something structural is wrong and in ones
        # when a real symbol is missing, so show enough to tell those apart.
        lines = out.splitlines()
        errs = [ln for ln in lines if "error" in ln.lower()]
        print("\n".join(lines[:6]))
        print("... %d error lines; first 25:" % len(errs))
        print("\n".join(errs[:25]))
        sys.exit(res.returncode)

    print(out.strip())
    adopt_retail_header(OUT)
    print("\nlinked -> %s (%d bytes), retail import tables and versions adopted"
          % (OUT, os.path.getsize(OUT)))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

```

`tools/paths.py`:

```py
# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
"""Where the tools find the retail executable: MCM2_EXE, or the copy in the Ghidra workspace."""
import os

RETAIL = os.environ.get("MCM2_EXE", r"C:\mcm2ghidra\bin\Mcm2.exe")

```

`tools/pedump.py`:

```py
"""Read a PE32's headers straight from the file, field by field, and report what a port needs."""
import struct, sys, hashlib

CHARACTERISTICS = [
    (0x0001, "RELOCS_STRIPPED"), (0x0002, "EXECUTABLE_IMAGE"), (0x0004, "LINE_NUMS_STRIPPED"),
    (0x0008, "LOCAL_SYMS_STRIPPED"), (0x0020, "LARGE_ADDRESS_AWARE"), (0x0100, "32BIT_MACHINE"),
    (0x0200, "DEBUG_STRIPPED"), (0x1000, "SYSTEM"), (0x2000, "DLL"),
]
DIRS = ["EXPORT", "IMPORT", "RESOURCE", "EXCEPTION", "SECURITY", "BASERELOC", "DEBUG",
        "ARCHITECTURE", "GLOBALPTR", "TLS", "LOAD_CONFIG", "BOUND_IMPORT", "IAT",
        "DELAY_IMPORT", "COM_DESCRIPTOR", "RESERVED"]

path = sys.argv[1]
d = open(path, "rb").read()
print("file      : %s" % path)
print("size      : %d bytes" % len(d))
print("sha256    : %s" % hashlib.sha256(d).hexdigest())
e_lfanew = struct.unpack_from("<I", d, 0x3C)[0]
assert d[e_lfanew:e_lfanew+4] == b"PE\0\0", "not a PE"
coff = e_lfanew + 4
machine, nsec, tstamp, _psym, _nsym, optsz, chars = struct.unpack_from("<HHIIIHH", d, coff)
print("machine   : 0x%04X %s" % (machine, {0x14C: "i386"}.get(machine, "?")))
print("sections  : %d" % nsec)
print("timestamp : 0x%08X" % tstamp)
print("charact.  : 0x%04X  %s" % (chars, " ".join(n for b, n in CHARACTERISTICS if chars & b)))
opt = coff + 20
magic = struct.unpack_from("<H", d, opt)[0]
print("opt magic : 0x%04X %s" % (magic, {0x10B: "PE32", 0x20B: "PE32+"}.get(magic, "?")))
lmaj, lmin = d[opt+2], d[opt+3]
(szcode, szinit, szuninit, entry, basecode, basedata, imgbase) = struct.unpack_from("<IIIIIII", d, opt+4)
salign, falign = struct.unpack_from("<II", d, opt+32)
szimage, szhdrs = struct.unpack_from("<II", d, opt+56)
subsys = struct.unpack_from("<H", d, opt+68)[0]
ndirs = struct.unpack_from("<I", d, opt+92)[0]
print("linker    : %d.%d" % (lmaj, lmin))
print("imagebase : 0x%08X" % imgbase)
print("entry RVA : 0x%08X  (VA 0x%08X)" % (entry, imgbase + entry))
print("sect align: 0x%X   file align: 0x%X" % (salign, falign))
print("size image: 0x%X   size hdrs: 0x%X" % (szimage, szhdrs))
print("subsystem : %d %s" % (subsys, {2: "WINDOWS_GUI", 3: "WINDOWS_CUI"}.get(subsys, "?")))
print("data dirs : %d" % ndirs)
dd = opt + 96
for i in range(ndirs):
    rva, sz = struct.unpack_from("<II", d, dd + i*8)
    if rva or sz:
        print("   %-14s rva 0x%08X  size 0x%X" % (DIRS[i] if i < len(DIRS) else str(i), rva, sz))
    elif i in (5,):
        print("   %-14s ABSENT (rva 0, size 0)" % DIRS[i])
sh = opt + optsz
print("sections:")
print("   %-9s %-10s %-10s %-10s %-10s %s" % ("name", "VA", "VSize", "RawPtr", "RawSize", "flags"))
for i in range(nsec):
    o = sh + i*40
    name = d[o:o+8].rstrip(b"\0").decode("latin-1")
    vsz, va, rsz, rptr = struct.unpack_from("<IIII", d, o+8)
    fl = struct.unpack_from("<I", d, o+36)[0]
    print("   %-9s 0x%08X 0x%08X 0x%08X 0x%08X 0x%08X" % (name, imgbase+va, vsz, rptr, rsz, fl))

```

`tools/port.py`:

```py
# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
"""Hand the game's functions over to their C ports.

A C function marked `PORT(0x0044C1D0)` replaces the game's function at that address. This renames
the original in game.asm to orig_<name> - its bytes stay exactly where they are, so nothing in
.text moves - and declares the old name external, so every call to it and every vtable slot that
names it binds to the C instead (tools/link.py does the binding). The original stays callable from
C as game_orig_<name>, for comparison or as a fallback.

A port is refused if code elsewhere jumps to a label inside the function: those jumps would still
land in the original, half of it running under the port.

The exporter writes a relative call or jump as its opcode bytes and `dd target - ($ + 4)`, which
MASM cannot assemble once the target is external. Those become `call`, `jmp` or the conditional
jump instead, which MASM encodes at the same full length for an external target - so no byte
moves, and tools/verify.py checks that it did not.

It works from game.asm.pristine, the export as ExportAsm wrote it, so running it again is safe.
Writes build/ports.tsv - address, the game's name, the C name - for link.py and verify_text.py.
"""
import glob
import os
import re
import sys

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
ROOT = os.path.dirname(HERE)
SRC = os.path.join(ROOT, "code", "mcm2")
ASM = os.environ.get("MCM2_ASM", r"C:\mcm2ghidra\out\game.asm")
PRISTINE = ASM + ".pristine"
PORTS = os.path.join(ROOT, "build", "ports.tsv")

# PORT(0x0044C1D0) then the definition: the name is the last word before the parameter list.
MARK = re.compile(r"\bPORT\(\s*0x([0-9A-Fa-f]+)\s*\)\s*\n[^;{]*?\b(\w+)\s*\(", re.MULTILINE)
LABEL = re.compile(r"^(\S+):$")


# Conditional jumps by their second opcode byte, 0F 80 .. 0F 8F.
CONDITIONS = ["jo", "jno", "jb", "jae", "je", "jne", "jbe", "ja",
              "js", "jns", "jp", "jnp", "jl", "jge", "jle", "jg"]


def branches_as_instructions(lines, name):
    """Rewrite each `db <opcode>` + `dd name - ($ + 4)` pair as the instruction it encodes."""
    target = "    dd %s - ($ + 4)" % name
    for n in range(1, len(lines)):
        if lines[n] != target:
            continue
        opcode = lines[n - 1].strip()
        if opcode == "db 0E8h":
            mnemonic = "call"
        elif opcode == "db 0E9h":
            mnemonic = "jmp"
        else:
            m = re.match(r"^db 00?Fh, 0?8([0-9A-F])h$", opcode)
            if not m:
                sys.exit("cannot port %s: line %d refers to it after `%s`" % (name, n + 1, opcode))
            mnemonic = CONDITIONS[int(m.group(1), 16)]
        lines[n - 1] = "    %s %s" % (mnemonic, name)
        lines[n] = None
    lines[:] = [l for l in lines if l is not None]


def symbol(name):
    """A pattern for `name` as a whole symbol. Mangled names hold ? and @, which \\b does not see."""
    return r"(?<![\w?@$])%s(?![\w?@$])" % re.escape(name)


def ports_in_source():
    found = {}
    for path in sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(SRC, "*.c"))):
        with open(path, encoding="utf-8") as f:
            for m in MARK.finditer(f.read()):
                address = int(m.group(1), 16)
                if address in found:
                    sys.exit("0x%08X is ported twice: %s and %s" % (address, found[address], m.group(2)))
                found[address] = m.group(2)
    return found


def find_function(lines, address):
    """(name, first line, line after ENDP) of the function starting at `address`."""
    tag = "; @%08X" % address
    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
        if line != tag:
            continue
        j = i + 1
        if lines[j].startswith("PUBLIC "):
            j += 1
        name = lines[j].split()[0]
        if not lines[j].split()[1:2] == ["PROC"]:
            break
        for k in range(j + 1, len(lines)):
            if lines[k].startswith(name + " ENDP"):
                return name, i, k + 1
        break
    sys.exit("no function starts at 0x%08X in %s" % (address, ASM))


def main():
    ports = ports_in_source()
    with open(PRISTINE, encoding="utf-8") as f:
        lines = f.read().split("\n")

    rows, externs = [], []
    for address, c_name in sorted(ports.items()):
        name, start, end = find_function(lines, address)

        inside = {m.group(1) for line in lines[start:end] for m in [LABEL.match(line)] if m}
        if inside:
            pattern = re.compile("|".join(symbol(l) for l in inside))
            for n, line in enumerate(lines):
                if (n < start or n >= end) and pattern.search(line):
                    sys.exit("cannot port %s (0x%08X): line %d jumps inside it - %s"
                             % (name, address, n + 1, line.strip()))

        definition = re.compile(r"^(PUBLIC )?" + symbol(name))
        for n in range(start, end):
            lines[n] = definition.sub(lambda m: (m.group(1) or "") + "orig_" + name, lines[n])
        branches_as_instructions(lines, name)
        externs.append("EXTERN %s:PROC" % name)
        rows.append("%08X\t%s\t%s" % (address, name, c_name))

    if externs:
        at = lines.index("INCLUDE game_externs.inc") + 1
        lines[at:at] = ["; Ported to C (tools/port.py)"] + externs

    with open(ASM, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        f.write("\n".join(lines))

    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(PORTS), exist_ok=True)
    with open(PORTS, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        f.write("".join(r + "\n" for r in rows))
    print("%d function(s) ported" % len(rows))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

```

`tools/resources.py`:

```py
# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
"""Write Mcm2.exe's resources out as a .res file for the link.

Given a .res, the linker builds .rsrc itself and places it after every other section, fixing up
the RVAs inside it. That lets sections of our own sit between .data and .rsrc without moving
anything the game addresses: nothing in the game refers into .rsrc except through the resource
directory, which the header points at.
"""
import os
import struct
import sys

from paths import RETAIL

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
ROOT = os.path.dirname(HERE)

OUT = os.path.join(ROOT, "build", "game.res")


def resource_section(image):
    """(raw bytes of the resource section, its RVA)."""
    pe = struct.unpack_from("<I", image, 0x3C)[0]
    count, = struct.unpack_from("<H", image, pe + 6)
    opt_size, = struct.unpack_from("<H", image, pe + 20)
    rva, _ = struct.unpack_from("<II", image, pe + 24 + 96 + 8 * 2)
    for i in range(count):
        _, vsize, va, raw_size, raw_ptr = struct.unpack_from("<8sIIII", image, pe + 24 + opt_size + 40 * i)
        if va <= rva < va + vsize:
            return image[raw_ptr + rva - va:raw_ptr + raw_size], rva
    sys.exit("no resource section in %s" % RETAIL)


def entries(rsrc, offset):
    """(id or name, offset, is_directory) for each entry of the directory at `offset`."""
    named, numbered = struct.unpack_from("<HH", rsrc, offset + 12)
    for i in range(named + numbered):
        key, target = struct.unpack_from("<II", rsrc, offset + 16 + 8 * i)
        if key & 0x80000000:
            length, = struct.unpack_from("<H", rsrc, key & 0x7FFFFFFF)
            start = (key & 0x7FFFFFFF) + 2
            key = rsrc[start:start + 2 * length].decode("utf-16-le")
        yield key, target & 0x7FFFFFFF, bool(target & 0x80000000)


def res_name(key):
    """A type or name field: 0xFFFF and an ordinal, or a NUL-terminated UTF-16 string."""
    if isinstance(key, int):
        return struct.pack("<HH", 0xFFFF, key)
    return key.upper().encode("utf-16-le") + b"\0\0"


def pad4(data):
    return data + b"\0" * (-len(data) % 4)


def res_entry(type_key, name_key, language, data):
    names = pad4(res_name(type_key) + res_name(name_key))
    header_size = 8 + len(names) + 16
    header = struct.pack("<II", len(data), header_size) + names
    header += struct.pack("<IHHII", 0, 0x1030, language, 0, 0)   # MOVEABLE | PURE | DISCARDABLE
    return header + pad4(data)


def main():
    with open(RETAIL, "rb") as f:
        image = f.read()
    rsrc, base = resource_section(image)

    out = res_entry(0, 0, 0, b"")      # every .res opens with an empty entry
    count = 0
    for type_key, type_dir, _ in entries(rsrc, 0):
        for name_key, name_dir, _ in entries(rsrc, type_dir):
            for language, leaf, is_dir in entries(rsrc, name_dir):
                if is_dir:
                    sys.exit("unexpected fourth level in the resource tree")
                data_rva, size = struct.unpack_from("<II", rsrc, leaf)
                out += res_entry(type_key, name_key, language, rsrc[data_rva - base:data_rva - base + size])
                count += 1

    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(OUT), exist_ok=True)
    with open(OUT, "wb") as f:
        f.write(out)
    print("%d resources -> %s" % (count, OUT))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

```

`tools/verify.py`:

```py
# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
"""Check the build against retail: .text, .rdata and .data must match it byte for byte.

The one difference allowed is a reference to a ported function (build/ports.tsv): retail points it
at the function's address, the build at the C port in .open. That covers absolute pointers - vtable
slots, `push offset` - and relative calls and jumps. Every other differing byte fails the build,
including any in a ported function's original code, which stays in place untouched.
"""
import os
import struct
import sys

from paths import RETAIL

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
ROOT = os.path.dirname(HERE)
BUILT = os.environ.get("MCM2_BUILT", os.path.join(ROOT, "build", "OpenMCM2.exe"))
PORTS = os.path.join(ROOT, "build", "ports.tsv")
CHECKED = (".text", ".rdata", ".data")


def sections(path):
    """{name: (virtual address, bytes over the virtual size)}, with uninitialised tails as zeros."""
    with open(path, "rb") as f:
        image = f.read()
    pe = struct.unpack_from("<I", image, 0x3C)[0]
    count, = struct.unpack_from("<H", image, pe + 6)
    opt_size, = struct.unpack_from("<H", image, pe + 20)
    base, = struct.unpack_from("<I", image, pe + 24 + 28)
    out = {}
    for i in range(count):
        name, vsize, va, raw_size, raw = struct.unpack_from("<8sIIII", image, pe + 24 + opt_size + 40 * i)
        data = image[raw:raw + min(raw_size, vsize)]
        out[name.rstrip(b"\0").decode()] = (base + va, data + bytes(vsize - len(data)))
    return out


def main():
    retail, built = sections(RETAIL), sections(BUILT)
    open_start, open_bytes = built[".open"] if ".open" in built else (0, b"")
    in_open = lambda a: open_start <= a < open_start + len(open_bytes)

    ported = set()
    if os.path.exists(PORTS):
        with open(PORTS, encoding="utf-8") as f:
            ported = {int(line.split()[0], 16) for line in f if line.strip()}

    failed = False
    for name in CHECKED:
        va, old = retail[name]
        new = built[name][1][:len(old)]
        explained = bytearray(len(old))
        retargets = 0

        for i in range(len(old)):
            if old[i] == new[i] or explained[i]:
                continue
            for s in range(max(0, i - 3), min(i, len(old) - 4) + 1):
                was, now = struct.unpack_from("<I", old, s)[0], struct.unpack_from("<I", new, s)[0]
                absolute = was in ported and in_open(now)
                relative = ((va + s + 4 + was) & 0xFFFFFFFF) in ported and in_open((va + s + 4 + now) & 0xFFFFFFFF)
                if absolute or relative:
                    explained[s:s + 4] = b"\1\1\1\1"
                    retargets += 1
                    break

        unexplained = [i for i in range(len(old)) if old[i] != new[i] and not explained[i]]
        print("%-7s %8d bytes, %d references retargeted to ports, %d bytes unexplained"
              % (name, len(old), retargets, len(unexplained)))
        for i in unexplained[:10]:
            print("    0x%08X  retail %02X  built %02X" % (va + i, old[i], new[i]))
        failed |= bool(unexplained)

    if failed:
        sys.exit("the build differs from retail in ways no port explains")
    print("OK - the game's sections are retail's, apart from calls to the %d port(s)" % len(ported))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

```

`tools/verify_encodings.py`:

```py
# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
"""Decide, per instruction form, whether MASM can be trusted to reproduce retail's exact bytes.

WHY THIS EXISTS. ExportAsm.java currently emits the whole binary as `db` blobs - 99.76% of
game.asm is raw bytes, with only address operands symbolised. That is safe but unreadable, and it
is the single biggest thing standing between this repo and being reviewable by anyone else.

The obvious fix - emit `push ebp` instead of `db 055h` - is not obviously safe, and the reason is
x86 redundant encodings. `fadd st, st` is DC C0 in the retail binary, but MASM assembles that same
mnemonic to D8 C0. `add eax, 0` has a short form and a long form. `test edx, eax` and
`test eax, edx` differ only in the direction bit and disassemble to different operand orders. Any
of those silently changes an instruction length or content, which moves every address after it,
which breaks -FIXED -BASE:0x400000 and every layout anchor the build checks.

So the rule is: a mnemonic may be emitted ONLY if MASM assembles it back to the identical bytes.
This tool establishes that set empirically rather than by reasoning about the encoding tables.
Run it before changing the exporter, and after any toolchain upgrade - a new ml.exe is free to
pick different encodings, and this is what would notice.

Each candidate is assembled in its own 16-byte ALIGN slot. The longest x86-32 instruction is 15
bytes, so one wrongly-sized encoding cannot desync the candidates that follow it - without that, a
single early mismatch would report every later instruction as broken too.

Usage:
    py tools/verify_encodings.py                 # read the instruction stream out of game.asm
    py tools/verify_encodings.py --json out.json # also write the verdict per encoding
"""
import collections
import glob
import json
import os
import re
import struct
import subprocess
import sys

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
ROOT = os.path.dirname(HERE)
ASM = os.path.join(os.environ.get("MCM2_ASM_DIR", r"C:\mcm2ghidra\out"), "game.asm")

# Relative branches encode a displacement to a target that does not exist in a standalone test, so
# they cannot be round-tripped this way. They are also the one class the exporter already gets
# right, as `db <opcode>` followed by `dd <symbol> - ($ + 4)`.
#
# ONLY THE RELATIVE ONES. `call dword ptr [ecx + 54h]` is a vtable dispatch - no displacement to a
# label, nothing for the linker to rewrite, and the single most common call shape in this codebase.
# Excluding it along with `call 4049E0h` left every virtual call in the game as `db 0FFh, 051h,
# 054h`. capstone prints a relative target as a bare address and an indirect one as a register or
# memory operand, which is exactly the distinction needed.
BRANCH = re.compile(r"^(j[a-z]{1,3}|call|loop[a-z]*|xbegin)$")
REL_TARGET = re.compile(r"^0x[0-9a-fA-F]+$")


def is_relative_branch(mnemonic, operands):
    return bool(BRANCH.match(mnemonic)) and bool(REL_TARGET.match(operands.strip()))


HEXB = re.compile(r"0([0-9A-Fa-f]{2})h")


def instruction_stream(path, known=None):
    """Recover instruction boundaries from the exported assembly.

    The exporter writes one `db` line per instruction, with any symbolised operand on a following
    `dd` line, so the file itself carries the boundaries Ghidra established. Re-deriving them by
    linear disassembly would desync on the data-in-code this binary is full of.

    Since the exporter started emitting mnemonics, this file is no longer pure bytes, so a
    previously-verified mnemonic line is resolved back through `known` (data/encodings.tsv). That
    keeps the tool runnable against its own output instead of needing an all-`db` export kept aside
    somewhere: a form already in the table contributes its known bytes and is re-verified, and a
    form still emitted as `db` is tested for the first time. The set can only grow.
    """
    known = known or {}
    pure = collections.Counter()
    symbolic = 0
    in_code = False
    cur, cur_sym = bytearray(), False

    def flush():
        nonlocal cur, cur_sym, symbolic
        if cur:
            if cur_sym:
                symbolic += 1
            else:
                pure[bytes(cur)] += 1
        cur, cur_sym = bytearray(), False

    with open(path, encoding="latin-1") as f:
        for ln in f:
            s = ln.strip()
            if s == ".CODE":
                in_code = True
                continue
            if s.startswith(".DATA") or s == "END":
                in_code = False
            if not in_code or not s or s.startswith(";"):
                continue
            if (s.startswith("PUBLIC") or " PROC" in s or " ENDP" in s or s.endswith(":")
                    or s.startswith("ALIGN") or s.startswith("INCLUDE")):
                flush()
                continue
            if s.startswith("db "):
                body = s[3:]
                hexes = HEXB.findall(body)
                if hexes and not re.search(r"[A-Za-z_?@$][\w?@$]{2,}",
                                          re.sub(r"0[0-9A-Fa-f]{2}h", "", body)):
                    flush()
                    cur.extend(int(h, 16) for h in hexes)
                else:
                    cur_sym = True
                continue
            if s.startswith("dd ") or s.startswith("dw "):
                cur_sym = True
                continue
            if s in known:
                flush()
                pure[known[s]] += 1
                continue
            flush()
    flush()
    return pure, symbolic


# capstone prints operand spellings MASM rejects. None of these change which instruction is meant -
# they are spelling, and getting them right is what separates "MASM cannot parse this" from "MASM
# disagrees about the encoding". Those are very different findings and must not be conflated: the
# first is a gap in this converter, the second is a hard limit on what the exporter may emit.
STRING_OP = re.compile(r"^(?:(?:rep|repe|repne|repz|repnz)\s+)?(?:movs|stos|lods|scas|cmps)[bwd]$")
# The x87 arithmetic group, with or without the pop suffix. Deliberately not fld/fxch/fcom, which
# legitimately take a single st(n) operand in MASM and must not have `, st` appended.
X87_ARITH = re.compile(r"^f(?:add|sub|mul|div|subr|divr)p?$")
MNEMONIC_ALIAS = {"int3": "int 3", "pushal": "pushad", "popal": "popad"}


def to_masm(mnemonic, operands):
    def hexlit(m):
        v = m.group(1).upper()
        return ("0" + v if v[0].isalpha() else v) + "h"

    if mnemonic in MNEMONIC_ALIAS and not operands:
        return MNEMONIC_ALIAS[mnemonic]
    # `rep movsb byte ptr es:[edi], byte ptr [esi]` is just `rep movsb` to MASM, and a bare
    # `stosb byte ptr es:[edi], al` is just `stosb`; the operands are implicit in the opcode and
    # naming them is "too many operands to instruction".
    if STRING_OP.match(mnemonic):
        return mnemonic
    ops = re.sub(r"0x([0-9a-fA-F]+)", hexlit, operands)
    # capstone calls the 80-bit x87 memory operand `xword`; MASM only knows it as `tbyte`.
    ops = ops.replace("xword ptr", "tbyte ptr")
    # x87 arithmetic against a stack slot needs both operands spelled out in MASM, and the implied
    # destination depends on whether the instruction pops:
    #
    #   D8 CA  fmul  st(2)   ->  fmul st, st(2)      non-pop: destination is ST(0)
    #   DE C1  faddp st(1)   ->  faddp st(1), st     pop:     destination is ST(i)
    #
    # Getting this backwards still assembles - to the OTHER form, D8 CA becoming DC CA - so it
    # shows up as a silent re-encode rather than an error. That is exactly the failure this tool
    # exists to catch, and it caught it here.
    if X87_ARITH.match(mnemonic) and re.fullmatch(r"st\(\d\)", ops):
        ops = ops + ", st" if mnemonic.endswith("p") else "st, " + ops
    # MASM spells the implicit top-of-stack `st`, never `st(0)`.
    ops = re.sub(r"\bst\(0\)", "st", ops)
    return (mnemonic + " " + ops).strip() if ops else mnemonic


def find_ml():
    hits = sorted(glob.glob(r"C:\Program Files\Microsoft Visual Studio\*\*\VC\Tools\MSVC"
                            r"\*\bin\Hostx64\x86\ml.exe"))
    if not hits:
        sys.exit("could not find ml.exe")
    return hits[-1]


def object_text(path):
    """The .text bytes of a COFF object. MASM names the section .text$mn, not .text."""
    d = open(path, "rb").read()
    nsec = struct.unpack_from("<H", d, 2)[0]
    opt = struct.unpack_from("<H", d, 16)[0]
    for i in range(nsec):
        o = 20 + opt + i * 40
        if d[o:o + 8].rstrip(b"\x00").decode("latin-1").startswith(".text"):
            size, ptr = struct.unpack_from("<II", d, o + 16)
            return d[ptr:ptr + size]
    return b""


def main():
    try:
        import capstone
    except ImportError:
        sys.exit("needs capstone: py -m pip install capstone")

    if not os.path.exists(ASM):
        sys.exit("no game.asm at %s - run tools/ghidra/ExportAsm.java first" % ASM)

    sys.path.insert(0, HERE)
    import encodings_table
    pure, symbolic = instruction_stream(ASM, encodings_table.load())
    md = capstone.Cs(capstone.CS_ARCH_X86, capstone.CS_MODE_32)

    cand, undecodable = [], 0
    for b, n in pure.items():
        ins = list(md.disasm(b, 0x400000))
        if len(ins) != 1 or ins[0].size != len(b):
            undecodable += n
            continue
        if is_relative_branch(ins[0].mnemonic, ins[0].op_str):
            continue
        cand.append({"b": b, "mn": ins[0].mnemonic, "n": n,
                     "text": to_masm(ins[0].mnemonic, ins[0].op_str), "ok": True})

    out = os.path.join(ROOT, "build")
    os.makedirs(out, exist_ok=True)
    asm_path = os.path.join(out, "encodings.asm")
    obj_path = os.path.join(out, "encodings.obj")
    ml = find_ml()
    # Must match game.asm's own prologue. ASSUME FS:NOTHING in particular is load-bearing: without
    # it every `mov eax, dword ptr fs:[0]` is rejected with "use of register assumed to ERROR", and
    # 2405 perfectly good instructions get blamed on MASM rather than on this header.
    header = [".686P", ".XMM", ".MODEL FLAT", "ASSUME FS:NOTHING", "ASSUME GS:NOTHING",
              "OPTION CASEMAP:NONE", "OPTION NOSCOPED", ".CODE"]

    # Anything MASM refuses is re-emitted as db so the 16-byte slots stay 1:1 with the candidate
    # list, then the assembly is retried - one rejected line otherwise aborts the whole run and
    # tells us nothing about the remaining forms.
    for _ in range(12):
        src, line_of = list(header), {}
        for i, c in enumerate(cand):
            src.append("ALIGN 16")
            line_of[len(src) + 1] = i
            src.append("    " + (c["text"] if c["ok"]
                                 else "db " + ",".join("0%02Xh" % x for x in c["b"])))
        src += ["ALIGN 16", "END"]
        open(asm_path, "w").write("\n".join(src) + "\n")
        r = subprocess.run([ml, "-c", "-coff", "-Fo" + obj_path, asm_path],
                           cwd=out, capture_output=True, text=True)
        errs = re.findall(r"encodings\.asm\((\d+)\)\s*:\s*error", r.stdout + r.stderr)
        hit = [line_of[int(e)] for e in errs if int(e) in line_of]
        if not hit:
            break
        for i in hit:
            cand[i]["ok"] = False

    text = object_text(obj_path)
    if not text:
        sys.exit("ml.exe produced no .text")

    good = collections.Counter()
    reencoded = collections.Counter()
    unparsed = collections.Counter()
    examples, verdict = {}, {}
    for slot, c in enumerate(cand):
        got = text[slot * 16: slot * 16 + len(c["b"])]
        key = c["b"].hex()
        if not c["ok"]:
            unparsed[c["mn"]] += c["n"]
            verdict[key] = "db:syntax"
        elif got == c["b"]:
            good[c["mn"]] += c["n"]
            verdict[key] = "mnemonic"
        else:
            reencoded[c["mn"]] += c["n"]
            verdict[key] = "db:reencoded"
            examples.setdefault(c["mn"], (key, got.hex(), c["text"]))

    a = sum(good.values())
    b_ = sum(reencoded.values())
    c_ = sum(unparsed.values())
    total = a + b_ + c_
    print("instructions carrying a symbol   : %8d  (already emitted as db + dd <sym>)" % symbolic)
    print("instructions not decodable       : %8d  (data-in-code; must stay db)" % undecodable)
    print("testable instruction occurrences : %8d  in %d unique forms\n" % (total, len(cand)))
    print("  %-32s %8d  %6.2f%%" % ("emit as mnemonic (byte-identical)", a, 100.0 * a / total))
    print("  %-32s %8d  %6.2f%%" % ("must stay db (MASM re-encodes)", b_, 100.0 * b_ / total))
    print("  %-32s %8d  %6.2f%%" % ("must stay db (syntax unsupported)", c_, 100.0 * c_ / total))

    if reencoded:
        print("\nforms MASM re-encodes - these can never be emitted as mnemonics:")
        for k, v in reencoded.most_common(15):
            retail, masm, txt = examples[k]
            print("   %-9s %7d  retail=%-20s masm=%-20s  %s" % (k, v, retail, masm, txt[:40]))
    if unparsed:
        print("\nforms MASM would not parse (fixable in to_masm, then re-run):")
        for k, v in unparsed.most_common(15):
            print("   %-9s %7d" % (k, v))

    if "--json" in sys.argv:
        dest = sys.argv[sys.argv.index("--json") + 1]
        json.dump(verdict, open(dest, "w"))
        print("\nwrote per-encoding verdict for %d forms to %s" % (len(verdict), dest))

    if "--table" in sys.argv:
        # The exporter must emit the EXACT text that was verified here, not its own rendering of
        # the same instruction. Ghidra and capstone disagree about operand spelling, and a
        # differently-spelled mnemonic is an unverified one - it may well assemble to other bytes.
        # So the text travels with the verdict rather than being regenerated on the Java side.
        dest = sys.argv[sys.argv.index("--table") + 1]
        with open(dest, "w", newline="\n") as f:
            f.write("# instruction bytes -> MASM text, verified byte-identical by "
                    "tools/verify_encodings.py.\n")
            f.write("# Regenerate with: py tools/verify_encodings.py --table %s\n"
                    % os.path.relpath(dest, ROOT).replace("\\", "/"))
            for c in cand:
                if verdict.get(c["b"].hex()) == "mnemonic":
                    f.write("%s\t%s\n" % (c["b"].hex(), c["text"]))
        print("wrote %d verified encodings to %s" % (a and sum(1 for c in cand
              if verdict.get(c["b"].hex()) == "mnemonic"), dest))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

```
